# hana_rubric v1 — JSON keymap file

> **Status: IMPLEMENTATION PLAN — phased, delegate-ready.** Builds `hana_rubric` (JSONC keymap file, reflect-backed command registry, schema generation, hot reload) plus the keystroke/multistroke primitives it needs in `bevy_kana`, then migrates hana onto them.

Same for `#[reflect(Component)]` → `ReflectComponent`, `#[reflect(Default)]` → `ReflectDefault`. Phase 3 shipped broken on exactly this.
  - **`#[derive(Reflect)]` on a validated newtype is a hole — add `#[reflect(opaque)]`.** Derived reflection is structural and ignores Rust field privacy: safe code reaches a private inner field through `TupleStruct::field_mut(0)`/`Struct::field_mut` and writes a value the constructor would have rejected. `CommandId` carries `#[reflect(opaque)]` plus a `ReflectRef::Opaque` regression test for this reason; every later validated newtype needs the same pair.

## Gates

Gates are events outside this plan. A phase stamped `**Blocked by:**` cannot be dispatched until its gate clears.

| Gate | Blocks | Cleared by |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `G1` | every hana-side phase (14–20) | **Superseded — do not wait for a merge.** This gate was written as "`init/hana_catalyst` lands on `hana/main`". It has since been settled the other way: hana work happens **on that branch, in a separate checkout at `/Users/natemccoy/rust/hana_tool_graph`** (confirmed on branch `init/hana_catalyst`). Every hana-side phase targets that working copy directly. The branch has already rewritten `crates/hana/src/input/global_shortcuts.rs` (+283/−4), `escape.rs` (+18/−2), `mod.rs` (+10), and unified hana's git deps to rev `df97b2b`, which is why the work goes there rather than to `main`. |
| `G2` | hana consuming `hana_rubric` and the new `bevy_kana` types (15–20) | The git-rev pin landing (Phase 15). **Decided: pin, not publish** — following the `hana_prosody` precedent. |
| `G3` | — **resolved**, see below | Folded into the specs of Phases 4a, 18, and 19. No longer blocking. |

**G3 resolution, folded into the plan.** Catalyst's `PermanentShortcutsContext` **is** D8's mechanism and a better one than the design had: commands that must stay reachable live in a `bevy_enhanced_input` context the keymap system never reads, rather than the loader special-casing individual chords. D8's loader-rejection survives as the diagnostic emitted when a keymap file names a command belonging to that context.

- `PermanentShortcutsContext` **stays in hana** (`crates/hana/src/input/`) — it is a `bevy_enhanced_input` context (marker component plus `Actions::<PermanentShortcutsContext>` spawn). It does **not** move into `hana_rubric`.
- What crosses the boundary is only the *declaration* that a command is unremappable, carried by `command!` as a capability. The registry knows the command cannot be bound from a file; the loader rejects any entry naming it. The library never learns what an input context is, so nateroids gets the same facility without inheriting hana's context structure. **hana owns the binding; `hana_rubric` owns the refusal.**
- **Held-key state has exactly one owner: `keymap/runtime.rs`.** Catalyst's `sync_global_shortcuts_activity` keeps only its IME half — deactivate the context while a `hana_diegetic::ImeInputBlocker` session is open, a whole-context concern the matcher has no view of — and **drops its "hold inactive until every held shortcut key is released" condition**, which duplicates the runtime's held-source refcounts and already-down-key inhibition. Context activity answers "should this context see input at all"; the runtime answers "given input, what matches, and what is still held."

### Phase 1 — `bevy_kana`: `Keystroke`, `Modifiers`, and the chord parser · status: done (`523c3471`)

#### Work Order

**Goal:** `"cmd-shift-p"` parses to a canonical `Keystroke` carrying a bevy `KeyCode` plus a modifier set, and renders back to a canonical string.

**Spec:**

Create `crates/bevy_kana/src/input/keystroke/` with `mod.rs` and `sequence.rs`. Add `mod keystroke;` to `crates/bevy_kana/src/input/mod.rs` and re-export the public types there, from `lib.rs`, and from the prelude.

**Not feature-gated.** Both intended consumers (hana, nateroids) need keyboard input, so gating buys nothing, and it costs nothing to ungate: keystroke parsing produces bevy `KeyCode` + modifiers and needs `bevy_input`, **not** `bevy_enhanced_input`. Ungating does not drag the optional dep into the default build. `keybindings.rs` stays behind the `input` feature, since it genuinely uses `bevy_enhanced_input`.

`mod.rs` holds `Keystroke` (the module-name type, so it lives at the root) and `Modifiers`. `Modifiers` stays in `mod.rs` under the field-type exception — you cannot read `Keystroke` without it. This is the one permitted deviation from "module roots are table-of-contents only."

- `Modifiers` — a set of `{ control, alt, shift, platform }`, represented as a **`bitflags` set, not four `bool` fields**. `bitflags = "2"` is already a `[workspace.dependencies]` entry (MIT OR Apache-2.0, so the dual-license invariant holds); `bevy_kana` adds `bitflags.workspace = true` and the root manifest needs no edit. One `u8` gives a single-compare `Eq` and a single-write `Hash` — `Keystroke` is the key of Phase 2's prefix index, so both run on every key edge — and the parser accumulates modifiers by `insert`ing rather than assigning four fields. `platform` is the Cmd/Ctrl abstraction; reuse the existing `PlatformShortcutMode` in `keybindings.rs:209-223` (`Command` on macOS, `Control` elsewhere, selected by `current()`) as the normalization authority rather than duplicating the `cfg!` test. **Canonicalization is a masking step at construction**: the `PLATFORM` flag is set only on macOS, and a platform-modifier token folds into `CONTROL` elsewhere, so `platform-p == ctrl-p` off macOS and `platform-p != ctrl-p` on it are true by construction rather than by a comparison special case. Uncanonical flag combinations must be unconstructible from outside the module — keep the `bitflags` type's raw constructors out of the public surface.
- `Keystroke` — `{ modifiers: Modifiers, key: KeyCode }`. Comparison and hashing are on the **canonical** value, never on source text.
- **Parser** — a tokenizer over a fixed modifier vocabulary, splitting on `-`, last token is the key. Accepted modifier spellings, all normalizing to the same canonical value: `cmd`, `super`, `win`, `platform` → platform modifier; `ctrl`, `control`; `alt`, `opt`, `option`; `shift`. Modifier order in the source string is irrelevant (`shift-cmd-p` == `cmd-shift-p`); canonical `Display` order is fixed (suggested: platform, ctrl, alt, shift, key).
- Left/right modifier spellings must not be able to slip through as distinct — normalize them to the same canonical modifier.
- Key-name vocabulary maps to `bevy::input::keyboard::KeyCode`. Cover letters, digits, function keys, arrows, and the named keys hana already binds (see `global_shortcuts.rs` reference for the in-use set). Unknown key names are a parse error naming the offending token, never a panic.
- Errors are a typed `KeystrokeParseError` carrying the offending token and its byte offset within the input string — Phase 6 needs the offset for source-located diagnostics.

`sequence.rs` holds `KeystrokeSequence` — an ordered, **non-empty** sequence of `Keystroke`. The non-empty invariant is enforced by construction (private field, fallible constructor), not by convention. Parse from a space-separated string (`"g g"`, `"cmd-k l"`). `Display` round-trips.

**Write our own test cases.** Zed's tests are copyrightable; reimplementation is from observed behavior only.

**Files:**
- `crates/bevy_kana/src/input/keystroke/mod.rs` — create: `Keystroke`, `Modifiers`, parser, `Display`, `KeystrokeParseError`
- `crates/bevy_kana/src/input/keystroke/sequence.rs` — create: `KeystrokeSequence`
- `crates/bevy_kana/src/input/mod.rs` — add `mod keystroke;` and re-exports
- `crates/bevy_kana/src/lib.rs` — re-export; check whether the prelude needs updating
- `crates/bevy_kana/Cargo.toml` — add `bitflags.workspace = true` (unconditional, not under the `input` feature); ensure `bevy_input` (or the bevy facade path already in use) is available outside the `input` feature

**Constraints from prior phases:** none — this is Phase 1.

**Acceptance gate:** `verify.sh check bevy_kana`, `verify.sh test bevy_kana`, `verify.sh lint bevy_kana` all green. Tests prove: modifier-order aliases parse equal; `cmd`/`super`/`win` normalize identically; `ctrl-p` and the platform chord are distinct on macOS and equal on Linux/Windows; left/right modifier spellings collapse; `Display` round-trips every parsed value; unknown key names and empty input return typed errors with byte offsets; `KeystrokeSequence` cannot be constructed empty. `cargo tree -p bevy_kana --no-default-features -e normal` must show `bevy_enhanced_input` absent while `keystroke` still compiles. `-e normal` is required: `bevy_kana` dev-depends on `fairy_dust`, which depends back on `bevy_kana` with `features = ["input"]`, so the default edge set always reports the crate present.

### Retrospective

**What worked:**
- `bitflags` over four `bool`s, with canonicalization as a masking step at construction. `platform-p == ctrl-p` off macOS and `!=` on macOS both fall out of the representation; no comparison special case exists anywhere.
- The feature-gate restructure landed cleanly: `keystroke` is ungated, `action`/`bind_action_system`/`event`/`keybindings` moved behind `#[cfg(feature = "input")]`, and `bevy_enhanced_input` is absent from `bevy_kana`'s normal dependency edges with default features off.
- One `CANONICAL_KEY_MAPPINGS` table now backs parsing, `Display`, and the round-trip test, so a key added later cannot drift out of sync between them.

**What deviated from the plan:**
- **Added `pub const fn Keystroke::new(Modifiers, KeyCode)`**, which the Work Order did not list. Both reviews called its absence a blocker: Phase 2's matcher must build a `Keystroke` per key edge from a live `KeyCode` plus held modifiers, and with `FromStr` as the only route it would have had to format a string and reparse it — which also breaks Phase 2's no-allocation-on-the-match-path requirement. It takes an already-canonical `Modifiers`, so it cannot introduce an uncanonical value. User-approved.
- `PlatformShortcutMode` was **moved** out of `keybindings.rs` into its own module, `crates/bevy_kana/src/input/platform_shortcut_mode.rs`, as `pub(super)` rather than referenced in place — `keystroke` is ungated and `keybindings` is not, so the ungated module could not depend on the gated one. Its `cfg!(target_os = "macos")` body is unchanged; `keybindings.rs` now imports it.
- `crates/bevy_kana/Cargo.toml` needed `bevy = { features = ["bevy_log", "keyboard"] }` — the plan anticipated only `bitflags`. `keyboard` must be unconditional, not under `input`, because ungated `keystroke` names `KeyCode`.

**Surprises:**
- **Every gate command refused to run, and the first delegate pass therefore coded blind.** `-p bevy_kana` was ambiguous: the workspace crate (`0.2.0-dev`) and a published `bevy_kana 0.1.0` were both in the graph, the latter reached through `fairy_dust` → `bevy_brp_extras 0.20.1` → crates.io. Cargo aborts on an ambiguous spec before compiling, so `verify.sh` reported nothing and the delegate reported success on code that did not compile. CI never caught it because CI names no package — it runs `--workspace`. Resolved permanently by publishing `bevy_kana 0.2.0` and a `bevy_brp_extras 0.22.2` that drops the dependency, then bumping the workspace pin (commit `eeacc29b`).
- `cargo tree`'s default edge set includes dev-dependencies, so the `bevy_enhanced_input`-absence check reported four hits from `bevy_kana`'s own dev-dep loop through `fairy_dust`. The gate line needed `-e normal`; as originally written it could never have passed.
- Feature unification hid a compile error: dev-dependencies pull `bevy` with a larger feature set, so a broken `KeyCode` import compiled under `test` and `clippy` but failed under `check --lib`.

**Implications for remaining phases:**
- Phase 2 constructs keystrokes with `Keystroke::new(modifiers, key)`; no format-then-reparse path is needed.
- `-p <package>` is now unambiguous workspace-wide, so every later phase's `verify.sh` lines work as written.
- `PlatformShortcutMode` is `pub(super)` within `input/` — reachable from `keybindings.rs`, not from outside `input/`. Anything in `hana_rubric` or `hana` needing the Cmd/Ctrl distinction must go through `Modifiers::with_platform`/`has_platform`, not the mode enum.
- Platform-split tests are `#[cfg]`-split and CI is Ubuntu-only, so the macOS half of the platform behavior never runs in CI. Recorded, deliberately not fixed.

### Phase 1 Review

An architect pass over Phases 2–20 returned 24 findings. Sixteen were mechanical
and went straight into the affected Work Orders; eight became decisions, filtered
down to eight `**Pending decision:**` blocks across six phases. Nothing was
rejected.

**Applied directly:**

- **Delegation Context** — `input/` is no longer flat and `keystroke` is *ungated*
  while its four siblings are `#[cfg(feature = "input")]`; every `keybindings.rs`
  line number shifted (~+2) and `PlatformShortcutMode` is no longer in that file;
  `bevy_kana/Cargo.toml` gained `bitflags` and `bevy` features. Every phase from 2
  on is dispatched with this section, so stale refs here poison all of them.
- **Delegation Context + Phase 3** — `serde` with `derive` added as a required new
  workspace dependency (`serde_json_lenient` does not supply `#[derive(Deserialize)]`);
  `bevy_state` reclassified from a fifth dependency to a `bevy` facade feature; the
  workspace's `default-features = false` pin on `bevy` recorded, so every member
  names its own features.
- **Phase 2** — `Keystroke::new` is `const` and takes canonical modifiers, so match
  paths build keystrokes directly instead of formatting and reparsing;
  `prelude.rs` added to Files; `KeystrokeSequence` needs `first()`/`len()`/`iter()`
  with an infallible `first()`, because the workspace's `unwrap_used`/`expect_used`/
  `unreachable` denies make `as_slice().first()` unhandleable.
- **Phase 2 + Phase 15** — `cargo tree` gates gain `-e normal`. Phase 1 hit a
  dev-dependency phantom (`bevy_kana` dev-depends on `fairy_dust`, which depends
  back with `features = ["input"]`), and CI's `--all-features` runs never re-check
  the absence.
- **Phase 3** — `clippy::cargo` is denied workspace-wide, so the new crate's
  `[package]` needs `description`, `keywords`, `categories`, `homepage`, and a
  `readme` that resolves; `README.md` added to Files.
- **Phase 4a + Phase 16** — `command!` call sites need `InputAction`, `Event`, and
  `Reflect` in scope unqualified, not just `ReflectKeymapCommand`: the wrapped `action!`
  and `event!` expand with no internal imports.
- **Phase 6** — binding parse yields `KeystrokeSequenceParseError`, not
  `KeystrokeParseError`; its offsets are already sequence-relative, so only the
  binding's span start is added. `KeystrokeParseError::at_offset` is `pub(super)`
  and unreachable from this crate.
- **Phases 6, 7, 8** — `Display` renders a fixed canonical order over already-folded
  modifiers, so an authored `cmd-p` reads back as `ctrl-p` off macOS. Diagnostics
  quote the preserved original text; tests asserting rendered keystroke text are
  platform-split.
- **Phases 9, 10** — every test must set the app-name parameter *and*
  `$XDG_CONFIG_HOME` to a temp directory and assert the resolved path is inside it.
  Without both, a test writes into the developer's real config directory.
- **Phase 17 + Phase 19** — `PlatformShortcutMode` is `pub(super)` and invisible to
  hana; platform folding is reached through `Modifiers::with_platform()`. The
  recovery chord becomes a plain `const Keystroke` with no `cfg!` and no runtime
  parse.

**Deferred as `**Pending decision:**` blocks** (each stops the `/plan:delegate`
pre-dispatch check at its phase):

- **Phase 2 A** — nothing converts held keys into `Modifiers`, and routing physical
  Super through `with_platform()` would make it a silent Ctrl alias off macOS.
  Proposed `Modifiers::from_pressed(&ButtonInput<KeyCode>)` in `bevy_kana`, where
  the platform rule is visible.
- **Phase 4a A — resolved.** `command!` cannot forward `#[reflect(KeymapCommand)]`
  through `event!` (the attribute lands ahead of its derive, which `-D warnings`
  makes fatal), so `event!` gains an optional `reflect:` fragment it splices into
  its own `#[reflect(...)]`. No dependency cycle: the identifier is a token and
  resolves at the call site.
- **Phase 4a B — resolved.** `hana_rubric` re-exports `bevy_kana`'s `action!` and
  `event!` at its root behind `#[doc(hidden)]`, and `command!` expands through
  `$crate::action!` / `$crate::event!`. Consumers need only `hana_rubric`.
- **Phase 4a C — resolved.** Generated command types stay `pub`; Phase 16's
  "command types become `pub(crate)`" sentence is deleted. `action!` and `event!`
  hardcode `pub struct` and gain no visibility parameter.
- **Phase 5 A** — `KeymapContext`'s `Resource` bound cannot be satisfied by Phase
  14's `States` enum (a `States` type is not a `Resource`; `State<S>` is), so Phase
  14's compile-time assertion would fail. Move `Resource` onto `for_context::<C>()`.
- **Phase 7 A — resolved.** The protected-chord assertions are platform-split on
  `cfg!(target_os = "macos")`: `ctrl-p` is rejected off macOS and **accepted** on it,
  where it is an ordinary bindable chord. Phase 19 carries the identical split.
- **Phase 7 B / Phase 12 A — resolved.** `.with_protected_keystroke(Keystroke)` on
  Phase 12's plugin builder is the route, accepting zero or more: hana owns the
  value and passes it in Phase 19, Phase 7 owns the enforcement.

### Phase 2 — `bevy_kana`: `SequenceMatcher<T>` · status: done (`27b33a1f`)

#### Work Order

**Goal:** a prefix-indexed matcher that consumes completed `Keystroke` values and yields opaque `T` indices, with pending state and timeout-driven resolution.

**Spec:**

Create `crates/bevy_kana/src/input/keystroke/sequence_matcher.rs` holding `SequenceMatcher<T>`, `MatchOutcome`, and `PendingSequence`. The file is named for its anchor type, not `matcher.rs`; `MatchOutcome` and `PendingSequence` are its return and field types.

**Domain-free.** The matcher knows nothing of JSON, commands, conditions, tombstones, or `World`. It returns opaque `T` and **takes its timeout as an input parameter** rather than owning a policy value.

**Input boundary — fixed now so deferred simultaneous chords stay additive.** `SequenceMatcher<T>` consumes *completed canonical `Keystroke` values*, never raw key edges. If simultaneous chords (`"j+k"`) are ever built, they arrive as one preprocessing stage beside it (`keystroke/chord.rs` + `keystroke/simultaneous_matcher.rs` emitting a completed `Keystroke`), and `sequence.rs` plus the prefix/timeout behavior are untouched. Had the matcher consumed key edges, adding chords would mean rewriting it, because simultaneity timing and sequence-prefix timing would share one state machine.

**Construction:** built from a set of `(KeystrokeSequence, T)` pairs into a prefix index keyed by canonical strokes. Construction is the only place that allocates or scans; matching does neither.

**Multistroke timing — the required behavior:**
- When both `g` and `g g` are live, **defer** `g`. Fire it on timeout.
- On a non-matching next stroke, **fire the deferred command and reprocess the unmatched stroke once from the root** — two physical strokes can therefore invoke two commands, which is the conventional behavior.
- Reload, a context change, and the recovery chord all **cancel pending state without firing the deferred command**. Expose this as an explicit `cancel_pending()` that discards rather than resolves.
- When no longer sequence survives, a complete short match fires immediately — no timeout wait.

`MatchOutcome` must distinguish at minimum: an immediate match (`T`), a deferred-but-complete match now pending a longer candidate, pending-with-no-match-yet, an unmatched stroke (with any deferred `T` to fire first, plus the stroke to reprocess), and no-match-and-nothing-pending.

**Input-path budget applies here:** on a key edge inspect only current prefix candidates; check the timeout only while a sequence is pending. No allocation, no string parsing, no full scan.

**Live keyboard state → `Modifiers`.** Phase 1 shipped `Modifiers` as a parse-and-compare type; nothing converts the keys currently held down into one, and Phase 11's per-frame runtime needs that on every key edge. Add it here, in `bevy_kana`, because this is the only crate that can see `PlatformShortcutMode` — `hana_rubric` reimplementing the rule would fork the canonicalization the whole design rests on.

Add `Modifiers::from_pressed(&ButtonInput<KeyCode>) -> Modifiers` to `keystroke/mod.rs`. It reads the modifier key pairs and applies the platform rule internally:
- Both platforms: `AltLeft`/`AltRight` → `ALT`, `ShiftLeft`/`ShiftRight` → `SHIFT`.
- macOS: `SuperLeft`/`SuperRight` → `PLATFORM`, `ControlLeft`/`ControlRight` → `CONTROL`.
- Elsewhere: `ControlLeft`/`ControlRight` → the platform modifier (which canonicalizes to `CONTROL`), and **physical Super contributes nothing** — it is not a keyboard modifier in this model. Phase 11 documents that so it reads as a decision rather than a surprise.

`bevy` is already a dependency of `bevy_kana`; `ButtonInput` and `KeyCode` are available without a feature change, and `keystroke` stays ungated.

**Files:**
- `crates/bevy_kana/src/input/keystroke/sequence_matcher.rs` — create
- `crates/bevy_kana/src/input/keystroke/mod.rs` — add `mod sequence_matcher;` and re-export `SequenceMatcher`, `MatchOutcome`. `PendingSequence` stays private unless it appears in the public outcome. Also add `Modifiers::from_pressed`.
- `crates/bevy_kana/src/input/keystroke/sequence.rs` — add the accessors `KeystrokeSequence` shipped without: `first()`, `len()`, and `iter()`. Phase 7 tests "first canonical stroke is the protected chord" and "a `Held` command is bound to exactly one stroke", and Phase 11 walks strokes; with only `as_slice()`, callers must write `as_slice().first()` and then handle a `None` arm that the non-empty invariant makes unreachable — which the workspace's `unwrap_used` / `expect_used` / `unreachable` denies forbid. `first()` is therefore infallible, returning `Keystroke`, justified by the invariant.
- `crates/bevy_kana/src/input/mod.rs`, `crates/bevy_kana/src/lib.rs`, `crates/bevy_kana/src/prelude.rs` — re-export. All three: Phase 1 put every keystroke type in all three places, and the prelude is how consumers import.

**Constraints from prior phases:**
- Phase 1 built `Keystroke` (canonical, `{ modifiers: Modifiers, key: KeyCode }`) and `KeystrokeSequence` (non-empty by construction) in `crates/bevy_kana/src/input/keystroke/`. The module is **not** feature-gated; `keybindings.rs` remains behind `input`.
- Comparison and hashing are on canonical values, never source text — the prefix index keys on canonical strokes.
- **`Keystroke::new(Modifiers, KeyCode)` exists and is `const`.** It takes an already-canonical `Modifiers`, so it cannot produce an uncanonical value. Build match-path keystrokes with it — never by formatting a string and reparsing, which would allocate on the steady-state path this phase's budget forbids.
- `PlatformShortcutMode` lives in `crates/bevy_kana/src/input/platform_shortcut_mode.rs` as `pub(super)` — usable inside `crates/bevy_kana/src/input/`, invisible outside it. Reach platform semantics through `Modifiers::with_platform()` / `has_platform()`.

**Acceptance gate:** `verify.sh check|test|lint bevy_kana` green. Tests prove: `g` alone fires immediately when no longer sequence exists; `g` defers when `g g` is live and fires on timeout; `g` + a non-matching stroke fires `g` **and** reprocesses the stroke from the root, yielding two outcomes; `cancel_pending()` discards without firing; a three-stroke sequence resolves; two sequences sharing a two-stroke prefix disambiguate on the third. Assert no allocation on the steady-state match path (construct once, match many). For `from_pressed`: with `ControlLeft` down the result equals the modifiers of a parsed `"ctrl-p"` on every platform and additionally of a parsed `"cmd-p"` off macOS; with `SuperLeft` down the result carries the platform flag on macOS and is empty off it; alt and shift map on both platforms. Split those assertions on `cfg!(target_os = "macos")` the way Phase 1 split its own — CI is Ubuntu-only, so the macOS half never runs there. `cargo tree -p bevy_kana --no-default-features -e normal` must still show `bevy_enhanced_input` absent — this phase adds a file to the ungated `keystroke` module and could silently reintroduce the edge, and CI only ever runs `--workspace --all-features` (`.github/workflows/ci.yml:105,131,167`), so nothing else re-checks it.

### Retrospective

**What worked:**
- The prefix index is a flat `Vec<PrefixNode<T>>` addressed by a newtype index rather than `Box`ed nodes, so the whole trie is one allocation-run at construction and the match path is a `HashMap` lookup plus two `Option` writes. The allocation assertion passes.
- `Modifiers::from_pressed` landed exactly as the resolved decision specified; the `cfg!(target_os = "macos")` test split means the Ubuntu-only CI half is meaningful.

**What deviated from the plan:**
- `SequenceMatcher<T>` carries a `T: Copy` bound the Work Order did not name. The deferred value must be both returned in `MatchOutcome` and stored in `PendingSequence` in the same call. Opaque indices satisfy it; every later phase must pass a `Copy` type as `T`.
- `MatchOutcome::Reprocess` is a struct variant `{ deferred: Option<T>, keystroke: Keystroke }`, not a tuple. Phase 11 matches on it by field name.
- A test-only counting global allocator was added to support the no-allocation assertion, in a new `crates/bevy_kana/src/allocation_test_support.rs` behind `#[cfg(test)]`. It is not in the Work Order's Files list.

**Surprises:**
- **A deferred value is lost when the prefix walks deeper into a node with no value of its own.** With `g` and `g g g` both bound, `g g` followed by timeout or a mismatching stroke dropped `g` entirely. The fix threads a `carried: Option<T>` through `outcome_for_prefix`. The Work Order's acceptance tests did not cover this shape — every test case used `g`/`g g`, where the second node *does* hold a value. **Any later phase adding sequences must keep the deeper-prefix case tested.**
- The workspace's denied lint set fires on ordinary accessor shapes. `len()` needs `#[expect(clippy::len_without_is_empty, reason = …)]` because the type is non-empty by construction; `iter()` requires an `IntoIterator for &KeystrokeSequence` impl; both `len()` and `cancel_pending()` must be `const fn`. `#[expect]` is preferred over `#[allow]`, but the enforced rule is `allow_attributes_without_reason = "deny"` (root `Cargo.toml:78`) — either attribute is legal so long as it carries `reason = "…"`.
- Two delegate reports claimed all three verification gates passed when the lint gate was failing — once with a use-after-move that meant the crate did not compile at all. **Re-run `verify.sh check|test|lint` in the orchestrator before accepting any phase.**

**Implications for remaining phases:**
- Phase 11's runtime consumes `MatchOutcome` — it must handle `Reprocess` by firing `deferred` and re-calling `match_keystroke` with the same stroke, and its `T` must be `Copy`.
- Phases 7 and 11 use `KeystrokeSequence::first()`; it is infallible and returns `Keystroke` by value.
- The `-D warnings` clippy surface is stricter than the plan assumed. Any phase adding a public `len`, `iter`, or trivially-const method should expect the same three lints.

---

### Phase 2 Review

Eighteen findings; fifteen edited straight into the remaining Work Orders, three deferred as `**Pending decision:**` blocks.

- **Delegation Context** — refreshed for what Phase 2 left on disk: all three `keystroke` files now exist; the new `crates/bevy_kana/src/allocation_test_support.rs` is a `#[cfg(test)]`-only counting `#[global_allocator]`, `pub(crate)`, invisible outside the crate; the full public keystroke surface (now including `SequenceMatcher` and `MatchOutcome`) is re-exported from `input/mod.rs`, `lib.rs`, and `prelude.rs`.
- **Phase 3** — Spec's "four new workspace dependencies" corrected to five (the bullet list already had five); Cargo.toml Files bullet states that no `[features]` section is needed.
- **Phase 6, Phase 8** — Constraints now record that a keystroke renders as `platform-p` on macOS, never `cmd-p` (the writer emits the literal `"platform-"`), so **neither** platform's rendering matches authored text. This is why diagnostics quote the preserved original.
- **Phase 7** — Constraints corrected: the matcher's timeout is a **per-call** argument, not a construction parameter, so `keymap/constants.rs` is consumed by Phase 11, not here. Added: the `T: Copy` bound sits on the struct, so the command-handle index must derive `Clone, Copy, Debug, Eq, PartialEq`; `first()`/`len()`/`iter()` signatures; `SequenceMatcher::new` silently last-wins on a duplicate sequence and reports nothing, so identities must be resolved to one edit before construction; only render a `Keystroke` that came from the parser or `CANONICAL_KEY_MAPPINGS` (`Display` returns `fmt::Error` otherwise, so `to_string()` panics). Acceptance gate gains the deeper-prefix carry case — with `g` and `g g g` live, `g g` plus a timeout still fires `g`. That is the exact bug Phase 2 shipped and had to fix.
- **Phase 11** — Constraints gain `resolve_timeout`'s signature and its every-frame obligation; the struct-variant `Reprocess { deferred, keystroke }` contract (fire `deferred` only when `Some`, then re-call `match_keystroke` exactly once; `Deferred(T)` must **not** dispatch); the `T: Copy` bound; the `Display` panic hazard; and `keymap/constants.rs` as a read dependency. Spec gains: skip key edges whose `KeyCode` is a modifier key — feeding one to `match_keystroke` destroys pending state; the clock is `std::time::Instant`, not Bevy `Time`, sourced from one place in `KeymapRuntime` so timeout tests advance it without sleeping; and `hana_rubric` must install its own `#[cfg(test)] #[global_allocator]` (it cannot reuse `bevy_kana`'s `pub(crate)` one) whose counter is `thread_local!`, so the allocation assertion must call the routing function directly rather than through `app.update()`.
- **Phase 13** — benchmarks removed entirely (user decision, 2026-07-30). The input-path budget is structural and Phases 11/12 already assert it with allocation counts; a wall-clock number would have cost a `criterion` dev-dependency plus a `bench_support` feature in Phase 3 just to reach the crate-private routing types. Phase 13 is now the editor-acceptance phase only.
- **Deferred to Phase 7** — whether `CompiledKeymap` can be immutable while holding matchers. It cannot: the matcher owns `pending` and every method takes `&mut self`.
- **Deferred to Phase 11** — how a test observes that a sequence is pending (nothing on the matcher reports it, and three cancellation gates depend on it), and whether physical Super becomes inert on Linux and Windows (it does, under the shipped `from_pressed`; today's `BlockBy` swallows that press).
- Phases 14, 18, and 20 came through clean; Phases 5, 9, and 10 needed no changes.

### Phase 3 — `hana_rubric`: crate skeleton, diagnostics, `CommandId` · status: done (`daa7c6c0`)

#### Work Order

**Goal:** the crate exists, builds, lints clean, and carries the two foundations everything else depends on — the durable diagnostic surface and the validated command-id type.

**Spec:**

Create `crates/hana_rubric/` — it joins the workspace automatically through `members = ["crates/*"]`, but needs its own `Cargo.toml` with `[lints] workspace = true` and the workspace `license`, `edition`, and `version` inherited via `.workspace = true`.

**`clippy::cargo` is denied workspace-wide**, so `[package]` must also carry `description`, `keywords`, `categories`, `homepage`, and `readme` — and `readme` must point at a file that exists. Mirror `crates/hana_lading/Cargo.toml`, which already satisfies this gate; a new crate that omits any of them fails `verify.sh lint hana_rubric` on its first run.

**Dependencies to add** (all are new to this workspace — add to `[workspace.dependencies]` in the root `Cargo.toml`, then reference with `.workspace = true`):
- `serde_json_lenient` — JSONC (comments, trailing commas). MIT; what Zed itself uses.
- `serde = { version = "1.0", features = ["derive"] }` — absent from `[workspace.dependencies]` today (only `hana_prosody` and `bevy_clerestory` declare it directly, both with `derive`). `serde_json_lenient` alone does not provide `#[derive(Deserialize)]`, which Phase 6's wire structs need.
- `notify` — file watching.
- `strum` — derive-only, MIT. **A public dependency**: it appears in the library's published bounds and downstream apps add it to their own `Cargo.toml` (R7).
- `dirs` — config-directory resolution. `bevy_clerestory` pins `dirs = "6.0"`; match it.
- Plus `bevy`, `bevy_enhanced_input`, and `bevy_kana` (path dep on the workspace member, with the `input` feature).

**`bevy_state` is not a separate dependency** — `bevy 0.19.0` exposes it as a facade feature (`bevy_state = ["bevy_internal/bevy_state"]`), and this workspace uses the facade rather than granular bevy crates. Add it to `hana_rubric`'s `bevy` feature list instead — it is a feature, not a dependency. The bullet list above is the complete set: **five** new workspace dependencies.

**Name `bevy`'s features explicitly.** The workspace pins `bevy = { default-features = false, features = ["reflect_auto_register"] }` (root `Cargo.toml:14`), so a bare `bevy.workspace = true` gets almost nothing — this is exactly what forced Phase 1 to add `features = ["bevy_log", "keyboard"]` to `bevy_kana` mid-phase. `hana_rubric` needs at minimum `std`, `keyboard` (Phase 11 reads `KeyCode`), `bevy_state` (`for_state_context::<C>()`, R8), and `bevy_log`.

`src/lib.rs` is a table of contents: `mod command; mod condition; mod keymap; mod disk;` stay **private**; everything public exits by re-export. `src/prelude.rs` follows the crate convention `bevy_kana` already sets — common caller imports only.

`src/diagnostic.rs` — `Diagnostic`, `DiagnosticKind`, and the durable load-failure resource.

**Foundational: it depends on nothing internal**, so every producer (parsing, registry validation, disk health, companion publication) can write to it without a cycle. **Acyclicity holds only if `Diagnostic` and `DiagnosticKind` store paths, byte ranges, line/column positions, original text, and suggested strings — never registry handles, `CommandId`, `ConditionName`, or typed keymap stages.** Store the id as a plain `String`.

Each diagnostic records: source path, byte range, 1-based line and column, block index, context, original keystroke text, command id, kind, and suggested replacements.

*Durable* means the resource persists until the next successful load rather than flashing past, so BRP can read it during debugging and the command palette can show "keymap: 1 error" once it exists. Make it queryable from day one — the planned in-app keymap editor lists everything currently invalid, and this resource is that section's data source. Derive `Reflect` and `#[reflect(Resource)]` so BRP can see it.

`src/command/mod.rs` — declarations and re-exports only. `src/command/id.rs` — `CommandId`:
- Validates exactly one `::` and snake_case segments (`selection::zoom_to`, `camera::home`).
- **One implementation shared by the declaration macro's const check and the runtime parser** — the macro validates at compile time, the loader at run time, and they must not drift. Write the validator as a `const fn` over bytes where possible so both paths call it.
- Namespaces in use, following the comment groupings in hana's `global_shortcuts.rs`: `camera`, `selection`, `dimension_lock`, `transport`, `scene`, `voice`, `spawn`, `inspect`.

**Files:**
- `crates/hana_rubric/Cargo.toml` — create. **No `[features]` section is needed.** The input-path budget is proven by the allocation assertions in Phases 11 and 12, not by benchmarks, so nothing outside the crate needs to reach `CompiledKeymap`, `KeymapRuntime`, or `route_input`.
- `crates/hana_rubric/README.md` — create; `clippy::cargo` requires the `readme` path to resolve
- `crates/hana_rubric/src/lib.rs` — create (table of contents)
- `crates/hana_rubric/src/prelude.rs` — create
- `crates/hana_rubric/src/diagnostic.rs` — create
- `crates/hana_rubric/src/command/mod.rs` — create
- `crates/hana_rubric/src/command/id.rs` — create
- `Cargo.toml` (workspace root) — add the five new `[workspace.dependencies]` entries

**Constraints from prior phases:**
- Phases 1–2 put `Keystroke`, `Modifiers`, `KeystrokeSequence`, `SequenceMatcher<T>`, `MatchOutcome` in `bevy_kana::input::keystroke`, re-exported from `bevy_kana`'s root and prelude. `hana_rubric` consumes them; it never reimplements them.

**Visibility scheme — settled, inherited by Phases 4a/4b/5/6/8/10/12:** `pub mod prelude;` plus `pub(crate)`/`pub(super)` items in nested files, matching what the nine existing members already do. `crates/bevy_kana/src/lib.rs:25` declares exactly this `pub mod prelude;`. No workspace-level visibility config is needed or wanted.

**Acceptance gate:** `verify.sh check|test|lint hana_rubric` green — `missing_docs = "deny"` means every public item needs a doc comment from the start. Tests prove: `CommandId` accepts `camera::home`, rejects zero and two `::`, rejects non-snake-case segments, rejects empty segments; the const path and runtime path agree on a shared table of cases. `Diagnostic` holds no type from `command`, `condition`, or `keymap` (assert structurally — this is the acyclicity invariant).

### Retrospective

**What worked:** the fast-path Work Order assembled with zero research and produced a compiling crate shape on the first pass — module layout, prelude, manifest metadata, and `CommandId`'s validator all landed as specified. `is_valid` as a `const fn` over bytes works: the test table drives both the const path and `TryFrom` from one array.

**What deviated from the plan:**
- **Files** listed seven files but `lib.rs` must declare `mod condition; mod keymap; mod disk;`, so three one-line doc stubs (`src/condition.rs`, `src/disk/mod.rs`, `src/keymap/mod.rs`) were created that the plan never named. Later phases populate them.
- `CommandIdParseError` is public and re-exported although the Spec never named it — `TryFrom`/`FromStr` require an error type.
- One fix pass was needed for six review findings.

**Surprises:**
- **`#[reflect(Resource)]` needs `ReflectResource` imported by name.** This workspace's files use explicit one-item-per-line imports rather than `use bevy::prelude::*`, so the attribute's expansion does not resolve. `crates/bevy_kana/src/cascade.rs:8` gets away with a glob import; `hana_rubric` cannot.
- **Derived `Reflect` on a validated newtype is a hole.** `#[derive(Reflect)]` on `pub struct CommandId(String)` is structural and ignores Rust privacy: safe code reaches the inner `String` through `TupleStruct::field_mut(0)` and writes text `is_valid` would reject. Fixed with `#[reflect(opaque)]` plus a `ReflectRef::Opaque` regression test. **Every validated newtype this plan adds later has the same hole by default.**
- **`strum = "0.28"` gives no derive macros.** Strum's defaults are `["std"]`; `strum_macros` is re-exported only under `features = ["derive"]`. The plan called strum "derive-only" but the bare entry could not have served that purpose. Root `Cargo.toml` now carries `features = ["derive"]`.
- The delegate reported all three gates green while the crate did not compile — the third such report in this plan. Orchestrator re-verification is load-bearing, not ceremony.

**Implications for remaining phases:**
- `CommandId` stores a `String`, so it cannot be built in a const context. `CommandId::is_valid` *is* `const fn`, so Phase 4a's `command!` macro can validate at compile time via `const _: () = assert!(CommandId::is_valid(#literal));` but must construct the value at runtime or hold `&'static str`.
- `CommandId` now reflects opaquely; anything embedding it in a reflected struct sees an opaque leaf in BRP, not nested fields. The regression test enforces this.
- `strum`'s `derive` feature is on workspace-wide — later phases can use `#[derive(EnumIter)]` and friends directly.
- Any later phase adding `#[reflect(Resource)]`, `#[reflect(Component)]`, or `#[reflect(Default)]` in `hana_rubric` must import the corresponding `Reflect*` type explicitly.

### Phase 3 Review

Twenty-one findings; all resolved. Mechanical edits went straight into the affected Work Orders; five decisions that change scope or public API are deferred as `**Pending decision:**` blocks.

**Promoted into `## Delegation Context`**, because delegates are dispatched with that section and never see retrospectives:
- The **orchestrator re-runs `verify.sh check|test|lint` itself before accepting any phase.** Phases 1, 2, and 3 each had a delegate report all three gates green on code that did not compile or did not lint — three for three.
- Reflect attributes (`#[reflect(Resource)]`, `#[reflect(Component)]`, `#[reflect(Default)]`) need the matching `Reflect*` type imported **by name** under this crate's explicit-import style.
- `#[derive(Reflect)]` on a validated newtype is structural and bypasses field privacy — pair it with `#[reflect(opaque)]` and a `ReflectRef::Opaque` test.
- The visibility scheme settled in Phase 3, moved out of Phase 3's Constraints where Phases 4a/4b/5/6/8/10/12 could not see it.
- `bevy_kana` is at `v0.3.0-dev`; Phase 1 published `0.2.0` to crates.io.
- `PlatformShortcutMode` lives at `crates/bevy_kana/src/input/platform_shortcut_mode.rs` — its own module, not `keystroke/mod.rs`. Corrected in the Delegation Context file list and Key-files bullet, and in Phases 2, 17, and 19.

**Work Order corrections:**
- **Phases 4a, 4b, 12, 15, 16** — the trait is `KeymapCommand` and the type data `ReflectKeymapCommand`; the plan still said `Command`/`ReflectCommand` in six places. Phase 4's Constraints had already settled the name.
- **Phase 4a/4b** — `CommandId` is not const-constructible (it wraps a `String`); the compile-time check is `const _: () = assert!(CommandId::is_valid("…"))`, the trait carries `&'static str`, the registry calls `try_from` at build time, and the error type is `CommandIdParseError`. Also: `CommandId` reflects opaquely and the attribute must stay.
- **Phase 6** — the full ten-field `Diagnostic` signature is written out, with the rule that every field is required and an absent value is the empty string, not `None`. `CommandIdParseError` is payload-free, so a malformed id contributes no position of its own.
- **Phases 6, 7, 8, 11** — four drifted `bevy_kana` line references corrected: `keystroke/mod.rs:167-168`→`173`, `:180`→`185`, `:120-122`→`118-127` (branch at 125), `sequence.rs:38`→`41`.
- **Phases 5, 6, 9** — Phase 3 shipped `condition.rs`, `disk/mod.rs`, and `keymap/mod.rs` as one-line doc stubs with `lib.rs` already declaring them, so those bullets now say "populate the existing stub" and the redundant `mod` declaration bullets are gone.
- **Phases 5, 10** — two "confirm the dependency" bullets became statements of fact: `strum` carries `features = ["derive"]`, `hana_rubric`'s `bevy` entry lists `bevy_log`/`bevy_state`/`keyboard`/`std`, and `notify = "8"` is present. Neither phase needs a manifest edit.
- **Phase 11** — the gate widens to `bevy_kana` if its pending decision resolves to option B, which edits `sequence_matcher.rs`.
- **Phases 11 and 15** — Phases 4a, 4c, and 11 are the phases permitted to edit `bevy_kana`, and all must land before Phase 15 computes the rev hana pins. Phase 4c relocates the whole `input` feature into `hana_rubric`, so after it lands `bevy_kana` holds no keymap surface and this constraint binds only `keystroke` and `math`.
- **Phase 12** — the plugin must call `init_resource::<KeymapLoadFailures>()`; nothing anywhere inserted it, so this phase's own acceptance gate could not pass. `src/diagnostic.rs` added to its Files.
- **Phase 15** — the version facts are stale (Phase 1 published `0.2.0`, the member is now `0.3.0-dev`), so the Spec now says to re-read hana's manifest and lockfile before dispatch rather than trusting the numbers quoted.

**Deferred as pending decisions** — each sits in the earliest affected phase and stops the delegate loop there:
- **Phase 4a, Decision D — resolved (add the message field and the variants).** `Diagnostic` has no message field and `DiagnosticKind`'s six variants do not cover what Phases 4, 6, and 7 reject; the enum is public API, so late additions break consumers. Recommendation: add `message: String` and extend the enum in one pass here.
- **Phase 4, Decision E — resolved (split).** Phase 4 is now **Phase 4a** (the `bevy_kana` `event!` extension, the `KeymapCommand` trait, `ReflectKeymapCommand`, `command!`, `Capability`, and the `Diagnostic`/`DiagnosticKind` extension) and **Phase 4b** (`CommandRegistry`, `CommandEntry`, startup validation, cached invocation). 4b consumes only 4a's public output.
- **Phase 8** — the Spec calls for reflect-driven schema generation, but the generator lives in `bevy_remote::schemas` and this crate's `bevy` features do not include it. Recommendation: hand-build the document with the `serde_json_lenient` already present, since the content is a runtime enumeration rather than a type shape.

---

### Phase 4a — `bevy_kana` `event!` extension, `KeymapCommand` trait, `command!` macro · status: done (`fd7e91e1`)

#### Work Order

**Goal:** one `command!` invocation declares an input action, a semantic event, an id, authored palette text, and a capability — and the declared metadata is readable from the type registry.

**Spec:**

**The registry holds semantic command *events*, not input actions (R2).** `"camera::home"` resolves to the `bevy_kana` `event!` type (e.g. `CameraHomeEvent`), not the `action!` input-action marker. The command events already derive `Reflect` with `#[reflect(Event)]`, so `ReflectEvent::trigger` fires them by name at runtime — the same mechanism BRP uses (`TypeRegistration::data::<ReflectEvent>()` then `ReflectEvent::trigger`, `bevy/crates/bevy_ecs/src/reflect/event.rs:67`; BRP's `process_remote_trigger_event_request`, `bevy/crates/bevy_remote/src/builtin_methods.rs:1480`).

**Do not plan on dynamically emitting `Start<A>`.** `bevy_enhanced_input::Start<A>` derives only `EntityEvent` — no `Reflect`, no `ReflectEvent` — and enhanced-input's dynamic `ActionFns` table is private. A registry of input actions has no supported way to invoke what it resolved.

**1. Extend `bevy_kana`'s `event!` with an optional `reflect:` fragment** (settled Decision A, option 1).

`event!` (`crates/bevy_kana/src/input/event.rs:25`) matches `$(#[$meta:meta])*` **before** it writes `#[derive(Event, Reflect, Default)]`, so an attribute forwarded by a wrapping macro lands *ahead* of the derive that defines the `reflect` helper. That is the `legacy_derive_helpers` future-incompatibility warning, and this workspace denies warnings — a forwarded `#[reflect(KeymapCommand)]` will not compile. The fix is an arm that splices extra entries into the `#[reflect(...)]` list `event!` writes itself:

```rust
($(#[$meta:meta])* $event:ident, reflect: $($extra:ident),+ $(,)?) => {
    $(#[$meta])*
    #[derive(Event, Reflect, Default)]
    #[reflect(Event, $($extra),+)]
    pub struct $event;
};
```

Add the matching payload-form arm. Keep both existing arms byte-identical.

**This creates no dependency cycle and needs no manifest change.** `$extra:ident` captures a bare token; `bevy_kana` never names `KeymapCommand`, never imports it, and its `Cargo.toml` is untouched. Name resolution happens after expansion, in the *invocation* module — which is in `hana_rubric` (and, from Phase 16 on, in hana), where `ReflectKeymapCommand` is in scope. This is the same property the existing arms already rely on: `event!` emits bare `#[derive(Event, Reflect, Default)]` with no imports of its own.

**Testing the new arm inside `bevy_kana` cannot use `KeymapCommand`** — that type is downstream and invisible here. Prove the splice compiles with a reflected trait `bevy_kana` can name, or a `#[reflect_trait]` declared in the test module.

**2. `src/command/reflect_command.rs`** — the `KeymapCommand` trait, the `ReflectKeymapCommand` type data, and the `command!` macro.

The trait lives here, not in `command/mod.rs`: the table-of-contents exception covers the module-name *type* and the types in its fields or enum payloads; a trait has no fields and does not anchor the directory in that structural sense. The trait clears the `dont-create-traits-for-single-implementations` bar on two counts — dozens of generated implementations plus a documented downstream extension contract.

**The metadata is associated consts, and `ReflectKeymapCommand` is hand-written type data — not `#[reflect_trait]`.** `#[reflect_trait]` generates a `ReflectKeymapCommand` of its own (which would collide by name) and its generated accessor works through `&dyn Reflect`, so it cannot read associated consts at all. Write the type data explicitly and populate it from the consts in a blanket `FromType`:

```rust
pub trait KeymapCommand: Reflect {
    /// Must satisfy `CommandId::is_valid`; checked at expansion by `command!`.
    const ID: &'static str;
    const TITLE: &'static str;
    const DESCRIPTION: &'static str;
    const CAPABILITY: Capability;
}

#[derive(Clone, Debug)]
pub struct ReflectKeymapCommand {
    pub id:          &'static str,
    pub title:       &'static str,
    pub description: &'static str,
    pub capability:  Capability,
}

impl<T: KeymapCommand> FromType<T> for ReflectKeymapCommand {
    fn from_type() -> Self {
        Self { id: T::ID, title: T::TITLE, description: T::DESCRIPTION, capability: T::CAPABILITY }
    }
}
```

**The macro names both types.** Stable `macro_rules!` cannot derive `CameraHomeEvent` from a supplied `CameraHome`, and R2 requires a distinct input action and semantic event, so both identifiers are supplied. Bevy's reflect derive resolves `#[reflect(KeymapCommand)]` through an unqualified `ReflectKeymapCommand` in the invocation module, so **that helper is public and imported at every call site**.

**`ReflectKeymapCommand` is not the only unqualified name the expansion needs.** `action!` and `event!` expand to bare `#[derive(InputAction)]` and `#[derive(Event, Reflect, Default)]` with **no internal imports** — they resolve those derive macros in the *invocation* module. So `command!`'s call sites must also have `InputAction`, `Event`, and `Reflect` in scope. State this in the macro's doc comment and cover it in Phase 16's constraints; a call site that imports only `command` and `ReflectKeymapCommand` fails to compile.

```rust
use bevy::prelude::{Event, Reflect};
use bevy_enhanced_input::prelude::InputAction;
use hana_rubric::{command, ReflectKeymapCommand};

command! {
    action:      CameraHome,
    event:       CameraHomeEvent,
    id:          "camera::home",
    title:       "Reset Camera to Home",
    description: "Return the camera to its default framing.",
}
```

**3. The expansion reaches `action!`/`event!` through `$crate`** (settled Decision B, option 2).

`$crate` in a `macro_rules!` expands to the crate where the macro was *defined*, so inside `hana_rubric`'s `command!` it is `hana_rubric` — it cannot reach `bevy_kana`. Re-export both macros from this crate's root and expand through `$crate`:

```rust
// crates/hana_rubric/src/lib.rs
#[doc(hidden)]
pub use bevy_kana::{action, event};
```

```rust
// inside command!'s expansion
$crate::action!($action);
$crate::event!($event, reflect: KeymapCommand);
```

`#[macro_export]` puts `action!`/`event!` at `bevy_kana`'s **crate root**, so the re-export path is `bevy_kana::{action, event}` — not `bevy_kana::input::…`. `#[doc(hidden)]` keeps them out of rendered docs while leaving them resolvable; `doc(hidden)` never affects name resolution. This is the settled visibility scheme's third bucket. The rejected alternative was expanding to `::bevy_kana::action!`, which would force every consuming crate to carry `bevy_kana` as a direct dependency under exactly that name and would put the device crate into `hana_rubric`'s public contract — the inverse of the D4 split.

Record the chosen form in `command!`'s doc comment.

- **title** → the palette row and the fuzzy-search target. **description** → the palette's secondary line and the JSON schema's hover text. Both required; Phase 4b's startup validation rejects a missing or empty one. **A `Held` command has no palette row** (Phase 4b, Decision E) — its title and description are still required, serving as the key-binding editor's label and the schema's hover text.
- `command!` lives here and **not** in `bevy_kana` because declared ids and `CommandId` are the keymap file's vocabulary; putting them in the device crate is what the D4 split exists to prevent. No cycle — this crate already depends on `bevy_kana`.
- `id` is const-validated at expansion: the macro emits `const _: () = assert!($crate::CommandId::is_valid($id));`. `CommandId` itself is **not** const-constructible (it wraps a `String`), which is why the trait carries `&'static str` and Phase 4b calls `CommandId::try_from` once at build time.
- Generated command types stay `pub` — `action!`/`event!` hardcode `pub struct` and `command!` adds no visibility parameter (settled Decision C, option 2; Phase 16's `pub(crate)` sentence is deleted).

**4. `src/command/capability.rs`** — `Capability`, a closed enum carried by the declaration and stored in Phase 4b's registry entry:
- `OneShot` — the default; binds to any `KeystrokeSequence`.
- `Held` — **binds to any single keystroke including modifiers** (`f`, `shift-f`, `cmd-shift-f`) and **not** to a multi-key sequence. Phase 7 rejects a held command bound to a sequence, per-binding at load, with a message naming the hold-to-act restriction. This is a property of the command carried by the registry, not a format limitation (R3).
- `Unremappable` — the command is reachable only through the app's permanently-registered bindings and may never be named in a keymap file (G3). Phase 7's loader rejects any entry naming it, with a diagnostic explaining that it is reserved for recovery. The library never learns what an input context is.

`Capability` must be `const`-constructible and usable as an associated const value, so derive `Clone, Copy, Debug, Eq, PartialEq, Reflect` and give it no payload.

**5. `src/diagnostic.rs` — extend the diagnostic surface** (settled Decision D).

Phase 3 shipped `Diagnostic` with ten fields and none of them holds prose; `suggestions` is machine-applicable replacement text, not explanation. Six later phases need to say *why* something was rejected. `DiagnosticKind` is public API, so its variants are added in two planned passes rather than incrementally.

- Add `pub message: String` to `Diagnostic` — the human-readable sentence. It is required like every other field; the absent value is the empty string. `suggestions` keeps its existing meaning and is unchanged.
- Add the registry-validation variants to `DiagnosticKind`, which Phase 4b produces: `DuplicateCommandId`, `MissingCommandTitle`, `MissingCommandDescription`.
- **Phase 7 adds the capability and reserved-chord variants** — `HeldCommandInSequence`, `UnremappableCommand`, `ReservedKeystroke`. Do not add them here.
- The existing six variants (`Syntax`, `Keystroke`, `Command`, `Context`, `Disk`, `Companion`) are unchanged. Phase 6's rejected `[id, args]` binding form reuses `Syntax`.
- Update the acyclicity test and Phase 6's quoted field list to match.

**Visibility:** `pub` — `command!` (at the crate root, where `#[macro_export]` puts it), `KeymapCommand`, `ReflectKeymapCommand`, `Capability`. `#[doc(hidden)] pub` — the `bevy_kana` macro re-exports and any other macro-support item downstream expansion must name.

**Files:**
- `crates/bevy_kana/src/input/event.rs` — add the optional `reflect:` arms (unit and payload forms)
- `crates/hana_rubric/src/command/reflect_command.rs` — create
- `crates/hana_rubric/src/command/capability.rs` — create
- `crates/hana_rubric/src/command/mod.rs` — declarations and re-exports
- `crates/hana_rubric/src/diagnostic.rs` — `message` field, three new `DiagnosticKind` variants
- `crates/hana_rubric/src/lib.rs`, `src/prelude.rs` — re-export the public surface; `#[doc(hidden)] pub use bevy_kana::{action, event};`

**Constraints from prior phases:**
- **Do not name the trait `Command`.** `Command` is in `bevy::prelude` (`bevy_ecs/src/lib.rs:96`), so a local `Command` trait is an E0659 ambiguity in any file doing `use bevy::prelude::*` — which is every Bevy file. Name it `KeymapCommand`, giving `#[reflect(KeymapCommand)]` and `ReflectKeymapCommand`. The nouns are unaffected: bevy has `Commands`/`EntityCommand`/`ParallelCommands` but no `CommandId`, `CommandRegistry`, or `CommandEntry`. Two alternatives were checked and rejected — `Action` collides with `bevy_enhanced_input`'s prelude (`Action<A>`, `InputAction`, `Actions`, `ActionOf`) *and* would name the semantic command the same as the input action it wraps; `Operation` collides with `bevy_lagrange::Operation` (`crates/bevy_lagrange/src/lib.rs:213`), which hana imports from Phase 16 on.
- Phase 3 built `CommandId` in `command/id.rs` with a shared const/runtime validator — `command!` calls the const path.
- **`CommandId` is not const-constructible.** It wraps a `String`, so it cannot be an associated `const` on the trait, and `command!`'s expansion cannot build one. What *is* `pub const fn` is `CommandId::is_valid(&str) -> bool`, so the compile-time check the macro emits is `const _: () = assert!(CommandId::is_valid("camera::home"));`. The failure type is `CommandIdParseError` — a payload-free unit struct.
- **`CommandId` reflects opaquely.** Phase 3 put `#[reflect(opaque)]` on it, with a `ReflectRef::Opaque` test locking that in, because derived reflection ignores field privacy and would otherwise let safe code write an invalid id through `TupleStruct::field_mut(0)`. Any reflected struct holding a `CommandId` sees an opaque leaf over BRP, not a nested string field. Do not remove the attribute.
- Phase 3's `Diagnostic` stores only plain data (paths, ranges, line/column, text, suggestions); the added `message` is also plain data. Diagnostics must not embed registry handles or `CommandId`.
- `bevy_kana`'s `event!` (`src/input/event.rs:25`) already emits `#[derive(Event, Reflect, Default)] #[reflect(Event)]`; `action!` (`src/input/action.rs:19`) emits `#[derive(InputAction)] #[action_output(bool)]`. Wrap both — do not duplicate them.
- **This phase, Phase 4c, and Phase 11 are the phases permitted to edit `bevy_kana`**; all must land before Phase 15 computes the rev hana pins. Phase 4c moves the `input` feature out of `bevy_kana` entirely, so what this constraint protects after 4c is only `keystroke` and `math`.
- Reflect attributes need the matching `Reflect*` type imported **by name** under this crate's explicit-import style — `#[reflect(Resource)]` needs `ReflectResource` in scope.

**Acceptance gate:** `verify.sh check|test|lint bevy_kana` green **and** `verify.sh check|test|lint hana_rubric` green. Tests prove: `event!`'s new arm splices extra entries into `#[reflect(...)]` and both original arms still expand unchanged; `command!` expands to a working action + event pair; the event's `ReflectKeymapCommand` type data is retrievable from a `TypeRegistry` and carries the declared id, title, description, and capability; a `Held` declaration and an `Unremappable` declaration each round-trip their capability through the type data; `Diagnostic` carries `message` and the three new `DiagnosticKind` variants exist. A call site missing `InputAction`, `Event`, `Reflect`, or `ReflectKeymapCommand` is documented as a compile error in the macro's doc comment.

### Retrospective

**What worked:**
- Single delegate pass, ~12 minutes, no fix pass. Blind review returned `APPROVE` with zero findings; my own review found zero blockers and zero minors.
- The Work Order's arm-ordering instruction (`reflect:` arms first) was correct as written — a `reflect:` invocation cannot fall through to a non-`reflect` arm, and the two original arms are byte-identical in the diff.

**What deviated from the plan:**
- Nothing material. The delegate reported one "deviation" — `command!` call sites must import `ReflectKeymapCommand` (alongside `Event`, `Reflect`, `ReflectEvent`, `InputAction`) unqualified — but that is the acceptance-gate line above, so it is a met requirement rather than a deviation.

**Surprises:**
- The cause of that import requirement is `bevy`'s `#[reflect(Trait)]` derive helper: it forms the type-data name by concatenating `Reflect` onto the trait ident **in the caller's scope**, and there is no fully-qualified form. It cannot be hidden inside the macro expansion.
- The `cascade` example — the smoke target used for Phases 1–3 — contains **no `event!` call sites**. The real ones live in `fairy_dust` and the `bevy_lagrange` examples. Phase 11 (the other phase permitted to edit `bevy_kana`) must smoke one of those instead.
- `const _: () = assert!(CommandId::is_valid($id));` passes the workspace `clippy::panic` deny — a const-context `assert!` is not flagged.
- `bevy_lagrange`'s `showcase` example carries `required-features = ["fit_overlay"]` and cannot be launched through `brp_launch`, which has no features argument. `programmatic_control` is the featureless `event!`-using target.

**Implications for remaining phases:**
- Phase 4b can rely on `ReflectKeymapCommand` being retrievable from a `TypeRegistry` by `TypeId`, carrying `id`, `title`, `description`, and `capability` as `&'static str` / `Capability`.
- Phase 16 must add all five unqualified imports to every module it migrates — `InputAction`, `Event`, `Reflect`, `ReflectEvent`, `ReflectKeymapCommand`. `hana_rubric::prelude::*` is not a substitute: it re-exports types only (19 since Phase 4c added the keystroke surface), not the `command!` macro and not the four bevy names. Phases 13, 19, and 20 carry the same constraint.
- `Diagnostic::message` is now a **required** field. Every construction site must supply it; use `String::new()` where no message applies.

### Phase 4a Review

Fifteen findings. Ten edited straight into the remaining Work Orders; five deferred as `**Pending decision:**` blocks across Phases 4b, 5, 16, and 17.

- **Phases 5, 7, 9, 10, 12** — each gained the constraint that `Diagnostic::message` is a required `String`, not an `Option`. Every construction site in those phases must supply it.
- **Phases 13, 16, 19, 20** — each gained the two `command!` call-site constraints: the five names that must be imported unqualified, and the fixed positional key order with `capability` last and optional.
- **Phase 7** — now explicitly owns the last three `DiagnosticKind` variants. Phase 4a took `DuplicateCommandId`, `MissingCommandTitle`, and `MissingCommandDescription` earlier than Decision D's schedule anticipated.
- **Phase 6** — corrected: it adds no `DiagnosticKind` variants of its own, and its coverage test asserts "all eleven" variants, not "all ten".
- **Phase 4b Spec** — corrected to validate `CommandId::try_from(entry.id)` against the value read out of the type registry rather than `T::ID`; the registry walks `&dyn Reflect` and never has the concrete type.
- **Phase 4b Spec** — emptiness is the only title/description failure checkable at scan time, so the validation list says so instead of implying a richer check.
- **Phase 4b → Phase 12** — the "every shipped default has a description" check moved to Phase 12, which is the first phase that knows the shipped default set.
- **Phase 11** — noted that no example in this workspace exercises `SequenceMatcher`, so its smoke target has to be chosen deliberately rather than inherited from earlier phases.
- **Phase 20** — noted that `command!` has no event-only arm: each route command also generates an `InputAction` type that nothing spawns. Accepted, not an oversight.
- **Deferred to Phase 4b** — how a `Held` command reaches its `Binding::Custom(CustomInput)`, since `CustomInput` is runtime-allocated and cannot live on the trait.
- **Deferred to Phase 4b** — whether the registry must diagnose hand-written `KeymapCommand` impls that bypass the macro's compile-time id check and `#[reflect(Event)]`.
- **Deferred to Phase 5** — which `DiagnosticKind` carries that phase's condition failures.
- **Deferred to Phase 16** — `ModifySelection` and `FreezeTracking` have no semantic event, but `command!` mandates one.
- **Deferred to Phase 17** — what happens to the 23 `bind_action_system!` call sites that phase strands.
