### Phase 11 — `hana_rubric`: the per-frame runtime · status: todo

#### Work Order

**Goal:** key edges route through the compiled matcher to command dispatch, with held-input driven through enhanced-input's custom-input mechanism and every held/pending invariant owned in one place.

**Spec:**

`src/keymap/runtime.rs` — `KeymapRuntime` and `route_input`.

**This file exists because the per-frame state has no other legal home:** held-source refcounts, inhibition, generation tracking, and the `Instant` clock have no home in a resource that is swapped wholesale — `CompiledKeymap` is replaced wholesale on reload (routing takes `ResMut`, never `Res`, because every `SequenceMatcher` method takes `&mut self`), `reload.rs` owns only the transaction, `registry.rs` owns invocation, and the plugin root must stay a table of contents. Without it, held-input and reload invariants scatter across three files.

**Scheduling:** `route_input` runs in `PreUpdate`, **after `InputSystems` and before `EnhancedInputSystems::Update`**.

**Modifier model — adopt Zed's two-bit representation (user decision, 2026-07-30).** Today `PLATFORM` means "the platform shortcut modifier" and is physical Super on macOS but physical **Control** everywhere else, which leaves no bit for physical Super off macOS, so `from_pressed` discards it (`crates/hana_rubric/src/keystroke/mod.rs:118-127`). Two consequences: Super+`g` on Linux produces a bare `g` and fires the `g` binding; and because `parse_modifier` maps `ctrl` to `Modifier::Control` on every platform (`keystroke/mod.rs:274-276`) while `from_pressed` never sets `CONTROL` off macOS, **a binding spelled `ctrl-g` can never match on Linux or Windows.** Replace the collapse with gpui's model, verified in `zed-industries/zed@main`:

- `PLATFORM` means the **physical** Cmd/Win/Super key on every platform, never discarded, never merged. (gpui `Modifiers::platform`, `crates/gpui/src/platform/keystroke.rs:466-471`.)
- `CONTROL` means the **physical** Control key on every platform.
- `cmd` / `super` / `win` / `platform` keep spelling `PLATFORM` on every platform (gpui `keystroke.rs:152-158`); `ctrl` / `control` keep spelling `CONTROL`.
- Add a **`secondary`** modifier token meaning "the platform's usual shortcut modifier" — `PLATFORM` on macOS, `CONTROL` elsewhere (gpui `keystroke.rs:143-150`). This is the cross-platform spelling and the only token whose meaning is platform-dependent.
- Matching stays exact equality over the whole canonical modifier set (gpui `keystroke.rs:112`). **No blocking mechanism and no platform branch in the routing hot path:** Super+`g` canonicalizes to `PLATFORM`+`g`, which is simply not equal to a bare `g`, so nothing fires. The suppression `spawn_key::<A>()`'s `BlockBy` used to provide falls out of equality.

`Display` gains `secondary` nowhere — it keeps emitting the canonical physical order `platform-`, `ctrl-`, `alt-`, `shift-`. `secondary` is a parse-side spelling only, exactly like `cmd`/`super`/`win`.

**Migration is free at this commit:** every `cmd-`/`ctrl-` spelling in the tree is in tests or plan docs; no shipped default binding depends on the collapse, and the protected recovery chord is not implemented yet (Phase 19 owns it). Two done-phase test expectations invert and must be updated in this phase: Phase 2's "`ctrl-p` and the platform chord are **equal** on Linux/Windows" becomes distinct on every platform, and any `merged.rs` protected-chord test that relies on `cmd-p` and `ctrl-p` colliding off macOS (`crates/hana_rubric/src/keymap/merged.rs:1080`, `:1155`) must respell its protected keystroke `secondary-p`. Phase 4c's "not one assertion may be edited" rule covered a pure relocation and does not bind here; this is a deliberate model change, and the inverted assertions are named above so the change is auditable rather than silent.

**It owns:** pending sequences, the current generation, held-source reference counts, inhibited already-down keys, context-change and recovery-chord cancellation, and `CustomInputs` writes.

**`bevy_enhanced_input` keeps the entire device layer.** Enhanced-input remains the sole implementation of physical binding, modifier sets and blocking, press/hold/tap/release conditions, dead zones and value modifiers, the action state machine and its `Start`/`Fire`/`Complete`/`Cancel` transitions, `require_reset` and first-activation suppression, alias reference counting, and `InputContext` priority. **None of it is reimplemented.**

**One-shot commands.** The existing `action!` → `Start<A>` → `bind_action_system!` → semantic event chain stays as the statically declared path. The keymap dispatches the **same** semantic event through the cached `ReflectEvent::trigger` handle, so downstream systems cannot distinguish the two routes.

**Held actions.** Each keeps **one statically spawned `Action<A>` bound to `Binding::Custom(id)`**, using enhanced-input's public custom-input mechanism — `CustomInput`, `CustomInputs::register_input()`, `Binding::Custom`, all verified present in 0.26.0 (`src/context/input_reader/custom.rs`, `src/binding.rs:112`). **The matcher writes that input's `ActionValue` before enhanced-input evaluates**, so `Start`/`Complete` pairing, `require_reset`, and blocking remain enhanced-input's job. **Remapping a held key changes which physical stroke drives the custom input — not the action entity.** This is what avoids the spurious `Complete`-then-restart that entity respawn causes while a key is held.

**Why entity respawn is wrong, verified from source** (`bevy_enhanced_input-0.26.0/src/context.rs:369` `reset_action`, `:553` `FirstActivation`): removing an action's `ActionOf<C>` resets it and emits `Complete` from `Fired`; removed bindings enter `PendingBindings` **only** when `require_reset` is set. Hana's own actions use `ActionSettings::default()`, so `require_reset` is false and only `bevy_kana`'s modifier actions set it. Consequence: despawning and rebuilding action entities while a key is held completes the held action and can immediately reactivate its replacement from the still-held key. **Keep action entities and held-input handles stable.**

**On a successful generation change or context change:**
- Cancel pending sequences **without firing a deferred binding**.
- Drive active old-generation held routes inactive so **exactly one `Complete`** is emitted.
- Clear held-source reference counts.
- **Inhibit already-down keys until physical release.**

The recovery chord also cancels pending state without firing the deferred command.

**Modifier key edges are not keystrokes.** Skip every key edge whose `KeyCode` is `ShiftLeft`/`ShiftRight`, `ControlLeft`/`ControlRight`, `AltLeft`/`AltRight`, or `SuperLeft`/`SuperRight`. Those contribute solely through `Modifiers::from_pressed`, and none of them appears in `CANONICAL_KEY_MAPPINGS` so none can ever be a bound key. Feeding one to `match_keystroke` while a sequence is pending finds no child at the current prefix, which returns `Reprocess` and **destroys the pending sequence** — so typing the second stroke of `cmd-k shift-l` would fire the deferred binding and cancel.

**Physical Super off macOS is inert — document it here as a decision.** `Modifiers::from_pressed`'s non-macOS branch reads only Control; Super is ignored (`crates/hana_rubric/src/keystroke/mod.rs:118-127 (branch at 125)`). On Linux and Windows, holding Super and pressing `g` therefore produces a **bare** `g` keystroke and fires the `g` binding — whereas `spawn_key::<A>()` applies `BlockBy` to all modifiers and swallows it today. Super is not a keyboard modifier in this model.

**Allocation measurement.** Phase 4c moved `allocation_test_support.rs` into `hana_rubric`, so **use the crate's existing `TEST_ALLOCATOR`** — the one the keystroke suites already count with. Do not install a second `#[cfg(test)] #[global_allocator]`; a test binary installs at most one, and a second declaration is a compile error, not a fallback. (Before Phase 4c this constraint read the opposite way: the allocator was `#[cfg(test)] pub(crate)` inside `bevy_kana` and unreachable from here.) Its counter is `thread_local!`, so the allocation assertion must call `route_input` **directly** rather than through `app.update()` — a system run on a Bevy task-pool worker thread is invisible to a count taken on the test thread.

**Input-path budget — enforced here:** no heap allocation, string parsing, reflection lookup, or full-keymap scan. Command and condition names were resolved to stable handles at load; sequences live in a prefix index keyed by canonical strokes; the active condition id is cached from state transitions; on a key edge inspect **only current prefix candidates**; check the timeout **only while a sequence is pending**.

**Files:**
- `crates/hana_rubric/src/keymap/runtime.rs` — create. **The directory form is pre-authorized: if this file crosses the workspace split threshold, ship `crates/hana_rubric/src/keymap/runtime/{mod.rs, …}` instead, with `mod.rs` holding declarations and re-exports only.** Expect to need it — this module owns pending-sequence state, generation tracking, held-source refcounts, already-down-key inhibition, `Binding::Custom` writes, and the exclusive-system dispatch path, which are independently testable clusters. Natural seams: key-edge assembly, held-source refcounts, dispatch. **`self_named_module_files` is denied**, so a split means there must be **no** `runtime.rs` beside the `runtime/` directory — Phase 10 hit exactly this and had to restructure mid-phase after writing a 1,042-line `worker.rs`; decide the shape before writing, not after.
- `crates/hana_rubric/src/keystroke/sequence_matcher.rs` — add `pub fn is_pending(&self) -> bool`, returning `self.pending.is_some()`. **This is the only observable this phase's and Phase 12's cancellation gates can assert against.** `resolve_timeout` returning `None` is ambiguous between not-pending, pending-not-yet-expired, and expired-with-nothing-deferred, so a cancellation test written against it passes whether the sequence was cleared or is still armed. Do not mirror the flag in `KeymapRuntime`: a gate asserting against a mirror proves the mirror works, not the matcher.
- `crates/hana_rubric/src/keymap/mod.rs` — declare; `pub(crate)` re-export of the system
- `crates/hana_rubric/src/keystroke/mod.rs` — the modifier-model change above: `from_pressed` sets `CONTROL` from physical Control and `PLATFORM` from physical Super on **every** platform (delete the `cfg!(target_os = "macos")` split at `:118-127`); `parse_modifier` gains a `"secondary"` arm resolving to `Modifier::Platform` on macOS and `Modifier::Control` elsewhere (`:272-284`); update the doc comment on `from_pressed`, which currently states the discarded-Super behavior
- `crates/hana_rubric/src/keymap/merged.rs` — respell the protected keystroke in the tests at `:1080` and `:1155` to `secondary-p` so they keep asserting the collision they were written for
- `crates/hana_rubric/src/command/reflect_command.rs` — add the `build` constructor to the `KeymapCommand` trait beside `hold_phase` (`:15`); the blanket `FromType<T>` impl calls it, which is what removes the `T: Default` bound that blocked the `dispatch: fn(&mut World)` route
- `crates/hana_rubric/src/command/action.rs` — `command!` emits the constructor per concrete type: the one-shot/unremappable arms a plain construction, the `held,` arm one that fills `phase: HoldPhase`
- `crates/hana_rubric/src/command/event.rs` — `event!`'s field arm gains whatever the constructor needs; the unit arm already derives `Default` (note: the **derive** only — no `#[reflect(Default)]` is added, since the trait constructor replaces the reflection route)
- `crates/hana_rubric/src/command/registry.rs` — `CommandEntry` caches one boxed prototype per command, built at load from the trait constructor; `invoke` dispatch passes `&*prototype`
- `crates/hana_rubric/src/keymap/schema.rs` — add `secondary` wherever the generated schema enumerates modifier spellings for editor completion (if it enumerates them; the Phase 8 generator may describe the keystroke field as a free string, in which case no edit)
- `crates/hana_rubric/src/keymap/constants.rs` — **delete one lint attribute.** `SEQUENCE_TIMEOUT` ships under `#[expect(dead_code, reason = …)]` (`crates/hana_rubric/src/keymap/constants.rs:5-8`) because nothing read it yet. This phase is its first caller, so remove the whole attribute in the same change — a fulfilled `expect` emits `unfulfilled_lint_expectation`, which `-D warnings` turns into an error and fails this phase's own gate.
- `crates/hana_rubric/src/condition.rs` — four edits this phase owns: add the `ActiveCondition::handle` accessor, move the `KeymapSystems` set configuration from `Update` to `PreUpdate`, move the two sync systems with it, and relax their system parameters to `Option<Res<…>>` (all four spelled out in the constraints below)

**Constraints from prior phases:**
- **This phase has no reason to touch `bevy_kana` at all.** Phase 4c moved `event!`, `action!`, `bind_action_system!`, `Keybindings`, **and the whole `keystroke` tree** into `hana_rubric`, so every edit this phase could want — including option B's `sequence_matcher.rs` change — is a same-crate `hana_rubric` edit. The old "Phases 4a and 11 are the last permitted to edit `bevy_kana`" rule is retired. Phase 15 still computes the git rev hana pins from `feature/rubric`'s tip.
- **In-crate `command!` / `action!` declarations warn where hana's do not.** rustc exempts expansions of *external*-crate macros from `dead_code`, but these macros are defined in `hana_rubric` now, so any test-only command declared inside `crates/hana_rubric/src/` is in-crate output and the lint fires. Put `#[allow(dead_code, reason = "…")]` on the `#[cfg(test)]` module or the individual declaration — **never inside a macro body**, which would leak the suppression into every consumer's test build. `command/registry.rs` and `command/reflect_command.rs` already carry exactly this allowance.
- Phase 2's `SequenceMatcher<T>` consumes **completed canonical `Keystroke` values, never raw key edges** — this file does the key-edge → `Keystroke` assembly. Its `cancel_pending()` discards without resolving; that is the call for reload, context change, and the recovery chord.
- **`SequenceMatcher<T: Copy>`** — the bound is on the struct itself (`crates/hana_rubric/src/keystroke/sequence_matcher.rs:14`), so even naming the type requires `Copy`. Phase 7's opaque command-handle index must derive `Clone, Copy, Debug, Eq, PartialEq`. **That handle is an index into `CompiledKeymap`'s resolved-command table, not a `CommandId`** — never reconstruct a `CommandId` on the input path to call `CommandRegistry::title`/`invoke`, which would hash a heap `String` per keystroke.
- **`route_input` is an exclusive system — `fn route_input(world: &mut World)`.** `CommandRegistry` is a `Resource` and `CommandRegistry::invoke(&self, &CommandId, &dyn PartialReflect, &mut World, &TypeRegistry)` needs `&mut World` at the same time, so no system can hold `Res<CommandRegistry>` and `&mut World` together. Reach it through `world.resource_scope::<CommandRegistry, _>(…)`, cloning `AppTypeRegistry` (an `Arc`) out of the world first and reading it inside the scope. The acceptance gate's zero-allocation assertion depends on this signature, since it calls `route_input` directly rather than through `app.update()`. Phase 12's `finish` and Phase 20's BRP method use the same pattern.
- **`resolve_timeout(&mut self, now: Instant, timeout: Duration) -> Option<T>`** (`sequence_matcher.rs:92`) is the **only** route by which a deferred binding fires on timeout. Call it every frame while a sequence may be pending; a returned `Some(T)` is the deferred binding and must be dispatched. `match_keystroke` also calls it internally and surfaces an expired deferral as `Reprocess { deferred: Some(_), .. }`.
- **`MatchOutcome::Reprocess { deferred, keystroke }` is a struct variant**, not a tuple. Fire `deferred` only when it is `Some` — it is `None` when the pending prefix carried no completed value — then call `match_keystroke` with the same `keystroke` **exactly once**. Both `Reprocess` paths leave `pending` cleared, so the second call starts at the root and cannot return `Reprocess` again; there is no loop. **`MatchOutcome::Deferred(T)` must not dispatch** — it fires later via `resolve_timeout` or as a `Reprocess` payload.
- **The matcher's clock is `std::time::Instant`, not Bevy `Time`** (`sequence_matcher.rs:140`). Source the `Instant` from one place inside `KeymapRuntime` so this phase's timeout test and Phase 12's parse-failure-during-a-pending-sequence test can advance it without sleeping. The timeout value itself comes from `keymap/constants.rs` and is passed **per call** — it is not a construction parameter.
- **Only render a `Keystroke` that came from the parser or from `CANONICAL_KEY_MAPPINGS`.** `Display` returns `fmt::Error` for any other `KeyCode` (`crates/hana_rubric/src/keystroke/mod.rs:185`), and `to_string()`/`format!` panic on a `Display` that errors.
- **Phase 7's `CompiledKeymap` is replaced wholesale on reload, never edited in place — take `ResMut`, never `Res`,** because every `SequenceMatcher` method takes `&mut self`. It carries a `Generation` and holds `matchers: HashMap<ConditionHandle, SequenceMatcher<CommandHandle>>` keyed by the same handle type `ActiveCondition` stores, **plus a separate `global: SequenceMatcher<CommandHandle>` field** (`crates/hana_rubric/src/keymap/compiled.rs:29-34`).
  - **Route through `compiled.global` when `ActiveCondition::handle()` is `None`.** That state is reachable every frame before context synchronization first runs, and permanently in any app that registers zero conditions — in which case `matchers` is empty and `global` is the only matcher there is.
  - **There is no fallback chain to walk.** `MergedKeymap::from_resolved_edits` pre-merges the global bindings into every per-condition matcher and then applies that context's tombstones (`crates/hana_rubric/src/keymap/merged.rs:396-430`), so a condition's matcher is already complete. Never consult `global` as a second lookup after a per-condition miss — a tombstone must suppress the global binding, and a fallback lookup would resurrect it.
  - **`global` is itself a matcher with its own pending state,** so the "cancel pending on the **previously** active context" rule in this phase's Goal treats global as one of those contexts: transitioning from no-condition into a condition must `cancel_pending()` on `global`, and back out of it must cancel on the condition's matcher.
- **Dispatch through `CompiledKeymap::invocation(CommandHandle) -> Option<Invocation>`** (`compiled.rs:90`), never through `CommandRegistry` by id. Phase 7's table holds `Vec<CommandEntry>` in `CommandRegistry::iter_entries()` order (id-sorted, reproducible); a `CommandHandle` is its index. No command name survives into the compiled form, which is the whole point of the table — resolving a handle back to a `CommandId` to look it up again reintroduces the per-keystroke string hash this design exists to remove.
- **`CompiledKeymap` has no `Default` and nothing inserts it before Phase 12's `commit_reload`** (`compiled.rs:29` derives only `Resource`; `from_merged` is the only constructor). Since `route_input` is an exclusive system, `world.resource::<CompiledKeymap>()` aborts on a missing resource, and the workspace denies `clippy::panic`. Apply the same rule this phase already applies to an uninitialized `ActiveCondition`: **an absent `CompiledKeymap` means no keymap is active — return without routing and without a diagnostic.** Test it.
- **`crates/hana_rubric/src/keymap/compiled.rs:3-6` carries `#![expect(dead_code, reason = "keymap routing reads compiled matchers after the input runtime is added")]`.** This phase is that reader, so the attribute becomes fulfilled and `unfulfilled_lint_expectation` fails this phase's own gate under `-D warnings` — the same trap `keymap/constants.rs:5-8` sets. Narrow or delete it in the same change. Narrowing is often the right move rather than deletion: a module-scope `expect` stays **fulfilled** while any single item in the file is still dead, so removing it wholesale can trade one lint error for another.
- Phase 5's `ActiveCondition` holds a registry-resolved handle updated by change detection; read that one resource — never scan contexts or compare strings. **This phase is `ActiveCondition`'s first and only reader, so four `condition.rs` edits belong here, not earlier.** Phase 5 deliberately left them out: an unused `pub(crate)` item trips `dead_code` under `-D warnings`, and a system moved before its reader exists cannot be tested.
  1. **Add the handle accessor.** `ActiveCondition { handle: Option<ConditionHandle> }` has a private field and only `pub const fn is_initialized(&self) -> bool` (`crates/hana_rubric/src/condition.rs`). Add `pub(crate) const fn handle(&self) -> Option<ConditionHandle>`; this file is a crate sibling and can call it. Phase 7 does **not** add this — it keys off `ConditionRegistry::resolve` and never reads the active value.
  2. **`is_initialized() == false` means "no keymap is active", not "a bug happened".** Phase 5 inserts `ActiveCondition` unconditionally — including when context registration failed and the registry is empty — precisely so `Res<ActiveCondition>` cannot panic on a missing resource. When it reads uninitialized, `route_input` returns without routing and without a diagnostic; the registration failure was already recorded in `KeymapLoadFailures` at startup. Test it.
  3. **Move `KeymapSystems` to `PreUpdate`.** Phase 5 shipped `register_context` configuring `(KeymapSystems::UpdateActiveCondition, KeymapSystems::Route).chain()` in **`Update`**, but this phase's Spec puts `route_input` in `PreUpdate` after `InputSystems` and before `EnhancedInputSystems::Update`, and Phase 12 orders `commit_reload` before it. Left as shipped, routing would read an `ActiveCondition` written a frame late — which defeats the stated reason the set is exported at all ("an app can guarantee its own context derivation runs before the keymap reads it") and makes this phase's own "a context change mid-sequence cancels" gate observe stale state. Move the `configure_sets` call **and** `sync_resource_condition` / `sync_state_condition` from `Update` to `PreUpdate`, keeping the chain order. Consequence to document: an app ordering against `KeymapSystems::UpdateActiveCondition` must schedule its context-derivation system in `PreUpdate` too.
  4. **Relax both sync systems' parameters to `Option<Res<…>>`.** As shipped they take `Res<C>` and `Res<State<C>>` unconditionally, and a `Res<T>` for an absent resource panics. Both absences are reachable: `bevy_state 0.19.0` removes `State<S>` from the world whenever a computed state's `compute` returns `None`, and Phase 14 builds hana's `InteractionContext` on computed states — so any frame with no applicable variant would panic. The resource form panics likewise if an app calls `for_context::<C>()` without inserting `C`. Take `Option<Res<…>>` and return early when `None`, leaving `ActiveCondition` at its last value.
- Phase 4b's registry exposes invocation through a **cached** `ReflectEvent` handle; the runtime triggers through the handle, never by name lookup.
- Phase 4a's `Held` capability means exactly one keystroke including modifiers; the merge in Phase 7 already rejected sequences for held commands, so the runtime can assume a single stroke drives a custom input.

- **No example in this workspace exercises `SequenceMatcher`**; the unit tests in `crates/hana_rubric/src/keystroke/sequence_matcher.rs` are its only coverage. If this phase touches `event!` again, the launchable smoke targets are `bevy_lagrange`'s `programmatic_control` (featureless) or any `fairy_dust` target — **never `cascade`**, which contains no `event!` call sites at all, and never `showcase`, which carries `required-features = ["fit_overlay"]` and cannot be launched through the BRP tool.
**Pending decision:**

**Decision: split Phase 11 into 11a / 11b / 11c, or dispatch it whole**

Actual problem:
Phase 11 is four independent workstreams sharing one acceptance gate. Its **Files** span nine files: the Zed modifier-model change (`keystroke/mod.rs`, `keymap/merged.rs` tests, `keymap/schema.rs`), the command-construction change (`command/{reflect_command,action,event,registry}.rs`), the four `condition.rs` edits, and `keymap/runtime.rs` itself — and its gate carries roughly twenty distinct assertions. Phase 10 was a *single* coherent subsystem and still overran its planned shape badly enough to need a mid-phase module split and a nine-finding fix pass. This phase has three visible seams before it starts.

What exists now:
- One Phase 11 Work Order, `status: todo`, with one gate covering all four workstreams.
- The three decisions resolved on 2026-07-30 (Zed's two-bit modifier model, the `build` constructor plus load-time prototype cache, `SequenceMatcher::is_pending()`) are all written into it and are not in question here — only how the work is packaged.

What should change:
- **11a — Zed modifier model.** `keystroke/mod.rs` (delete the `cfg!(target_os = "macos")` split, add the `"secondary"` arm), `keymap/merged.rs` protected-keystroke respell, `keymap/schema.rs` if affected. Entirely self-contained and independently gateable; it also fixes the latent bug where `ctrl-g` can never match off macOS.
- **11b — command construction.** The `build` constructor on `KeymapCommand` plus the load-time prototype cache across `command/{reflect_command,action,event,registry}.rs`. Note: 11b will need a temporary `expect(dead_code)` on the cached prototype until 11c reads it — the same fulfilled-`expect` trap Phase 10 cleared for Phase 9.
- **11c — the runtime.** `keymap/runtime.rs` (or `runtime/`) plus the four `condition.rs` edits. Depends on both 11a and 11b.

Recommendation:
Split. Phase 10's fix pass cost a full extra dispatch cycle, and its findings were concentrated in exactly the seams a smaller phase would have isolated. 11a in particular is small, independently valuable, and carries a real bug fix that should not wait behind the runtime.

**Why this is not applied automatically:** the current authorization window covers "phases 10 and 11". Splitting Phase 11 into three changes what that window covers, and phase scope and sequencing are the user's call — so this stops the loop at Phase 11's pre-dispatch check even though the window is open. That is intended; do not suppress it.

Approve the split, or dispatch Phase 11 whole?

**Acceptance gate:** `verify.sh check|test|lint hana_rubric` plus `verify.sh check fairy_dust` green. (`bevy_kana` is **not** in this gate: Phase 4c moved `sequence_matcher.rs` into `hana_rubric`, so even option B stays inside one crate. `fairy_dust` is here because this phase edits `hana_rubric`'s crate root and prelude, and `fairy_dust` is the normal-dependency consumer where a broken re-export surfaces.) Tests prove: a single-stroke binding triggers its semantic event; a two-stroke sequence triggers on the second stroke; a deferred short binding fires on timeout; a held command's `Binding::Custom` value is written before enhanced-input evaluates, producing exactly one `Start` and one `Complete` across a press/release; remapping a held key while it is **not** pressed changes which stroke drives it without touching the action entity; a context change mid-sequence cancels without firing **and leaves `is_pending()` false**; already-down keys are inhibited until released; **pressing a modifier key while a sequence is pending leaves the pending sequence intact** (`is_pending()` still true). **Command-construction tests:** a `OneShot` command dispatched through `route_input` delivers its event with no concrete type named at the call site (the failure this replaces: routing holds only a `CommandId`); an `Unremappable` command dispatches the same way; a `Held` command still compiles and registers although its event has no `Default`, proving the constructor is on the trait rather than behind a `Default` bound; a command's prototype is built exactly once at load, not per keystroke. **Modifier-model tests:** physical Super + `g` does not fire a bare `g` binding **on every platform** (this replaces the `BlockBy` suppression and needs no hot-path branch); `ctrl-g` matches a physical Control + `g` press on every platform (it cannot today off macOS); `secondary-g` matches physical Super + `g` on macOS and physical Control + `g` elsewhere — split on `cfg!(target_os = "macos")`, since CI is Ubuntu-only; `cmd-g`, `super-g`, and `win-g` all match physical Super + `g` on every platform and are **not** equal to `ctrl-g` anywhere. Assert zero allocation on the steady-state routing path. Also prove the four `condition.rs` edits above: an app whose context registration failed routes nothing and does not panic (`is_initialized()` false); a context change written by a `PreUpdate` system is visible to `route_input` **in the same frame**, not the next; and a state-backed context whose `compute` returns `None` for a frame leaves the app running with `ActiveCondition` unchanged.

- Phase 4b allocates one `CustomInput` per `Held` command and owns the `CommandId` → `CustomInput` lookup on `CommandRegistry`. The matcher reads that lookup — it does not allocate, and it does not build its own table. A held command's semantic `Begin`/`End` event writes the **same** input value, so the matcher is one of two producers feeding one consumer.

- **The matcher is not the only writer, and the other writer is outside this file's refcounts.** Phase 4b's `register_held_observer` observer writes the same `CustomInput` from the command's own `Begin`/`End` event, with a "no-op when already in the requested state" guard (`crates/hana_rubric/src/command/registry.rs`). Treat the event path as a **second held source** in the reference count rather than as a peer writer: the runtime's count is `physical_sources + event_sources`, and the input reads `true` iff the total is non-zero. Otherwise a scripted `HoldPhase::End` while the physical key is down kills the hold until the key is released and re-pressed. A generation or context change clears only the **physical** sources — an event-driven hold is not owned by the keymap and must survive a reload.

**Command construction — a `build` constructor on the `KeymapCommand` trait, plus a load-time prototype cache (user decision, 2026-07-30).**

`CommandRegistry::invoke` takes `&dyn PartialReflect` (`crates/hana_rubric/src/command/registry.rs`) and `ReflectEvent::trigger` has no value-free form, but `route_input` resolves a keystroke to an opaque handle and never holds a concrete type — so it has nothing to pass. This blocks `OneShot` and `Unremappable` dispatch, the core of this phase. Held commands are unaffected; the runtime writes their `CustomInput` directly.

The route that would otherwise be obvious — put a monomorphized `dispatch: fn(&mut World)` on `ReflectKeymapCommand`, the pattern Phase 4b established — **is blocked by a bound**: it is filled in by the blanket `impl<T: KeymapCommand> FromType<T>`, and that blanket impl cannot require `T: Default`, because the held field-arm event carries a `phase: HoldPhase` field with no default.

**Adopt gpui's shape** (`zed-industries/zed@main`, `crates/gpui/src/action.rs`), which does not have this problem:

- The `Action` trait itself declares a static constructor, `fn build(value: serde_json::Value) -> Result<Box<dyn Action>>` (`action.rs:134`), generated per concrete type by the derive macro.
- The registry stores it as a bare function pointer — `type ActionBuilder = fn(json) -> anyhow::Result<Box<dyn Action>>` (`action.rs:231`) — keyed by name, and `build_action` calls it with `{}` when the binding carries no payload (`action.rs:351-368`).
- **The `Default` bound never appears.** The requirement lives on the trait every action already implements, so no blanket impl has to prove anything about the type.

For hana that means: **add the constructor to the `KeymapCommand` trait**, which `command!` already implements per concrete type — it generates `hold_phase()` exactly that way (`crates/hana_rubric/src/command/reflect_command.rs:15`). The macro knows which arm it is expanding, so the one-shot arm emits a plain constructor and the held arm emits one that fills `phase`. The `ReflectKeymapCommand` blanket impl then simply calls it; the bound problem dissolves for the same reason it does not exist in gpui.

**Pair it with the prototype cache:** `CommandEntry` builds one boxed value per command when the keymap compiles and dispatch passes `&*prototype`. The per-keystroke path then performs no reflection lookup and no allocation, which this phase's zero-allocation assertion requires. The cache is not an alternative to the constructor — it is what the constructor feeds.

**Signature scope:** take **no arguments** now. hana's keymap schema has no per-binding parameter payload, and adding one is not in this phase. Choose the signature so a payload argument can be added later without touching every call site — that is the door gpui's `build(value)` leaves open, and leaving it ajar costs nothing here.

---

### Phase 12 — `hana_rubric`: plugin assembly and the reload transaction · status: todo

#### Work Order

**Goal:** `KeymapPlugin` wires every module together and a reload is a transaction over a compiled resource — never over ECS entities.

**Spec:**

`src/keymap_plugin.rs` — `KeymapPlugin`, its impl, and system ordering. The filename follows the anchor type, not the crate name. The plugin struct sits immediately after imports (the bevy-plugin-ownership exception to anchor-type ordering).

`src/keymap/reload.rs` — **the commit transaction only.**

**The plugin root wires all modules.** No module reaches upward. The dependency direction, which must hold:

```text
diagnostic          → nothing
command, condition  → diagnostic
disk                → diagnostic
keymap              → command, condition, diagnostic, keystroke
keymap/schema       → command, condition
```

`keymap → disk` is **deliberately absent.** The plugin root hands generated companion buffers to the worker, receives byte snapshots back, calls the pure keymap compiler, then invokes the runtime transaction.

**Public builder surface:**
- **`KeymapPlugin::new()`, then `.with_app_name(&str)`, `.with_defaults(&'static str)`** (`include_str!`-supplied by the consumer), **and `.with_protected_keystroke(Keystroke)`** (Decision A below, zero or more). Phase 17 calls exactly these names; they appear in no other phase, so this is where they are fixed.
- `for_state_context::<C>()` / `for_context::<C>()` from Phase 5.
- **The shipped builder discards everything, and fixing that is this phase's first job.** Phase 5's `KeymapPlugin` is a *unit* struct with no fields, and `for_context`/`for_state_context` take `self` by value and return a freshly built `ResourceContextPlugin`/`StateContextPlugin` carrying nothing from the receiver (`crates/hana_rubric/src/keymap_plugin.rs:16`, `:25-39`). Phase 17 chains `.with_app_name(…).with_defaults(…).for_state_context::<InteractionContext>()`, so under the shipped shape every configured value would be silently dropped at the terminal call and the app would start with no defaults and no app name. Give `KeymapPlugin` the fields the `with_*` methods set, and make the `impl Plugin` returned by `for_*_context` carry them — the terminal call is what gets added to the `App`, so it must be the thing that owns the configuration. Add a test that a value set through `with_app_name` is observable after the returned plugin's `build` runs.
- **Exactly one context source, enforced rather than documented.** `keymap_plugin.rs`'s doc comment says to choose one of `for_context` / `for_state_context`, but calling both adds two sync systems writing the same `ActiveCondition` in the same set with last-writer-wins and no diagnostic. Once the builder carries state, record which context source was chosen and reject the second call — a startup diagnostic, or a compile-time impossibility if the restructured builder can make the terminal call consume `self`. Test it.
- **`Plugin::finish` is also where the companion buffers are generated — no other phase names the caller.** Phase 8 returns the schema bytes through one `pub(crate)` entry point and Phase 9 explicitly never calls it, so `finish` calls it, after inserting the registry and after context registration, and hands both byte buffers to the Phase 10 worker's startup. `finish` is the correct point because bevy runs every plugin's `build()` before any plugin's `finish()`, which is the only barrier guaranteeing every command and context an application or third-party plugin registered is present. There is no startup `SystemSet` for this and applications need no sequencing hook: registration happens in their own `build()`, which is already complete by then. `finish` runs before `Startup`.
  - **`schema_bytes`'s shipped signature is `pub(crate) fn schema_bytes(&CommandRegistry, &ConditionRegistry) -> Result<Vec<u8>, serde_json_lenient::Error>`** (`crates/hana_rubric/src/keymap/schema.rs`). It is fallible and needs **both** registries in scope at the same moment; `clippy::unwrap_used` and `clippy::panic` are denied workspace-wide, so the `Err` arm records a diagnostic and continues without companion bytes rather than aborting. `ConditionRegistry` is already present — `register_context` `init_resource`s it during `build` (`crates/hana_rubric/src/condition.rs:280-282`) — while `CommandRegistry` is the value `initialize` returns and `finish` itself inserts, so generate the schema after that insertion.
  - **There is no single terminal plugin type to hang `finish` on.** `KeymapPlugin` is not a `Plugin`; `for_context` / `for_state_context` return `impl Plugin` backed by two distinct types, `ResourceContextPlugin<C>` and `StateContextPlugin<C>` (`crates/hana_rubric/src/keymap_plugin.rs:26-38`), and the terminal call is what the application adds to the `App`. Write the assembly once — registry initialization, schema and default generation, worker startup — as a shared crate-private routine and have both types' `finish` call it. Do not duplicate the body, and do not introduce a third plugin that both add: an application adds exactly one context source, so a shared *plugin* would either be added zero times or, if both context methods were called, twice.
  - **This phase owns a second `keymap/`-side generator for the reference file's comment header** (resolved 2026-07-30; Phase 8 shipped none and Phase 9 may not call one). A `pub(crate)` function alongside `schema_bytes` in `crates/hana_rubric/src/keymap/schema.rs` takes the embedded default text, `&ConditionRegistry`, and the protected keystrokes, and returns bytes: a JSONC comment block — the recovery chord, then one line per registered condition with its `#[strum(message)]` text — prepended to the embedded default **verbatim**. `finish` calls it in the same breath as `schema_bytes` and hands both buffers to the worker. It is the only discovery path for legal `"context"` values outside a schema-aware editor, and generating it is what stops it drifting as conditions and the protected chord change.
  - **Two lint attributes Phase 8 shipped become fulfilled the moment `finish` calls the entry point, and a fulfilled `expect` is a build error under `-D warnings`.** Delete `crates/hana_rubric/src/keymap/schema.rs`'s module-level `#![cfg_attr(not(test), expect(dead_code, …))]` and `crates/hana_rubric/src/keymap/mod.rs`'s `#[cfg_attr(not(test), expect(unused_imports, …))]` on the `schema_bytes` re-export as part of this phase's diff — the same mechanism as `ResolvedEdits` and `merged.rs` below.
- **`Plugin::finish` calls `CommandRegistry::initialize(app.world_mut())`** — Phase 4b's public construction entry point, which scans the type registry for `ReflectKeymapCommand`, allocates every held `CustomInput`, and registers the `Begin`/`End` observers — **and then inserts the returned registry as a resource.** `initialize` deliberately does not insert itself. It returns `Result<CommandRegistry, Vec<Diagnostic>>`; `clippy::panic` is denied workspace-wide, so the `Err` arm writes the diagnostics into `KeymapLoadFailures`, logs at error level, and the app still starts. `finish` separately rejects any **shipped default** that does not resolve against the built registry. See the pending decision on all-or-nothing validation below.

**Reload is a transaction over a compiled resource, not over ECS entities:**

Parse, validate, merge, and compile a candidate **with no world mutation**, then swap **one wholesale-replaced compiled-keymap resource in a single main-thread system ordered before `EnhancedInputSystems::Update`**. `reload::commit_reload` is ordered **before** `route_input` and **must be an exclusive system — `fn commit_reload(world: &mut World)` — never `ResMut` and never deferred `Commands`**. `ResMut` is not an option here, despite reading as the lighter choice: `CompiledKeymap` has no `Default` and nothing inserts it before the first commit, and a system whose `ResMut` resource is missing does not run at all — so the first commit would never happen and the app would route nothing forever. The first commit *inserts* the resource; later commits replace it. Use the `resource_scope` pattern Phases 11 and 20 already use.

**Two-tier failure (D7), mirroring Zed's `JsonParseFailure` vs `SomeFailedToLoad` split:**

| Failure | Behavior |
| --- | --- |
| Whole-file parse failure (missing brace, bad JSON) | **Keep the last good keymap running, untouched.** Report the error. |
| Per-binding failure (unknown command id, unparseable keystroke, unknown context) | **Apply the good bindings, report the rest.** |

Not all-or-nothing, because one typo costs the other 34 bindings. Not apply-whatever-parsed, because a brace error drops you to *zero* bindings mid-session, including whatever you would use to recover. Two-tier is the only option where the app stays usable through every intermediate state an editor writes — **the worst outcome of a bad save is that nothing changes and you are told why.**

Precisely: **a whole-file parse failure changes nothing — not the generation, not the pending sequence, not held actions.** A partial semantic failure **commits one complete generation of defaults plus every valid edit.**

**The plugin inserts the durable diagnostic resource.** `KeymapPlugin::build` calls `app.init_resource::<KeymapLoadFailures>()`. Phase 3 shipped the type with `#[derive(Default)]`; the **only** other insertion anywhere is Phase 5's registration-**failure** path (`crates/hana_rubric/src/condition.rs:294`), which never runs when registration succeeds. So the line here is load-bearing, not redundant: without it the resource is absent at runtime in every healthy app, and this phase's own acceptance gate ("the durable diagnostic resource is populated and readable after each") cannot pass. `init_resource` is idempotent, so the two call sites do not conflict. `KeymapLoadFailures` is `#[reflect(Resource)]`, so it is also visible over BRP once inserted.

Each `mod.rs` re-exports with an explicit qualifier (`pub(crate) use reload::commit_reload;`).

**Files:**
- `crates/hana_rubric/src/keymap_plugin.rs` — **extend; Phase 5 already created it.** It ships `KeymapPlugin` (unit struct, `new()`, `for_context::<C>()`, `for_state_context::<C>()`), `KeymapSystems`, and `Default`. The plugin struct already sits immediately after the imports — that layout instruction is satisfied, not pending.
- `crates/hana_rubric/src/keymap/reload.rs` — create
- `crates/hana_rubric/src/diagnostic.rs` — no structural change expected; `KeymapLoadFailures` is inserted from the plugin and written by the reload transaction
- `crates/hana_rubric/src/keymap/mod.rs`, `src/lib.rs`, `src/prelude.rs` — wire and re-export the public surface
- `crates/hana_rubric/src/disk/worker/channels.rs` — change `start_disk_worker` to take `&KeymapPaths` and the schema buffer as `Option<Vec<u8>>`; widen whatever `pub(super)` visibility the destructured-message call site needs
- `crates/hana_rubric/src/disk/worker/runtime.rs` — the `&KeymapPaths` signature change, and **delete its item-scoped `#[expect(dead_code, …)]`** as the entry point goes live
- `crates/hana_rubric/src/disk/paths.rs` — widen the three `#[cfg(test)]` test helpers from `pub(super)` to `pub(crate)` (see the isolation rule below)

**Constraints from prior phases:**
- **In-crate `command!` declarations warn where hana's do not.** rustc exempts expansions of *external*-crate macros from `dead_code`, but Phase 4c made `command!`/`action!` local to `hana_rubric`, so any test-only command declared inside `crates/hana_rubric/src/` is in-crate output and the lint fires. Put `#[allow(dead_code, reason = "…")]` on the `#[cfg(test)]` module or the declaration — **never inside a macro body**, which leaks the suppression into every consumer's test build. `command/registry.rs` and `command/reflect_command.rs` already carry this allowance.
- Phase 11's `route_input` runs in `PreUpdate` after `InputSystems`, before `EnhancedInputSystems::Update`; `commit_reload` is ordered before it.
- Phase 11 owns held-source refcounts, inhibition, and pending cancellation. On a generation change the transaction signals the runtime; it does **not** manipulate that state itself.
- **Phase 10's worker — the shipped API, verbatim. This phase is its only consumer.** It delivers byte snapshots through a bounded coalescing slot and never parses. It lives in `crates/hana_rubric/src/disk/worker/` (`mod.rs` declarations + re-exports, `channels.rs`, `runtime.rs`, `watch.rs`).
  - `pub(crate) fn start_disk_worker(…) -> DiskWorkerChannels` (`disk/worker/runtime.rs`) spawns a dedicated OS thread. `DiskWorkerChannels::take_message(&self) -> Option<DiskWorkerMessage>` is a **non-blocking** peek — call it once per frame and do nothing when it returns `None`.
  - **The message is tri-state and the third state is the deletion policy crossing the boundary.** `DiskWorkerMessage { snapshot: Option<DiskSnapshot>, diagnostics: Vec<Diagnostic>, … }` and `DiskSnapshot { source_path: PathBuf, contents: Option<Arc<[u8]>> }`:
    - `snapshot: None` → **diagnostics only, no keymap change.** Record them; do not touch the generation.
    - `snapshot: Some(contents: Some(bytes))` → new user-keymap bytes; run the transaction.
    - `snapshot: Some(contents: None)` → **confirmed deletion. Commit a defaults-only generation** — the user file is gone and its overrides go with it. This is not an error and produces no failure diagnostic.
  - **Resolve `KeymapPaths` once, on the app thread, and pass it in.** As shipped, `start_disk_worker` takes an app name and resolves `KeymapPaths` *inside* the spawned thread, while this phase separately needs `paths.default_keymap()` for `from_sources`. Two resolutions on two threads can disagree — a test that drops its `XdgConfigHome` guard before the worker thread reaches its resolution sends the worker into the developer's real `~/.config/<app>`. **Change the signature to take `&KeymapPaths`** (resolved once here), which also gives this phase the defaults path it needs and a single place to handle `KeymapPaths::new` returning `None` without racing the worker.
  - **Commit a defaults-only generation synchronously in `finish`, before starting the worker.** Otherwise input is inert from `finish` until the first disk read completes — and permanently if paths fail to resolve or the app never called `.with_app_name`, since Phase 11 rules that an absent `CompiledKeymap` means no routing and no diagnostic. With the synchronous first commit, the shipped default keymap is live from frame zero and every worker message is an ordinary reload. This also decouples the "unresolvable shipped default" gate case from disk timing.
  - **`take_message` may append one synthesized diagnostic** reading "N older disk diagnostics were discarded before delivery." It is stamped `DiagnosticKind::Disk` + `DiagnosticSeverity::Failure` and has **no source span and no originating binding** — every consumer that formats a diagnostic by pointing into a file must tolerate that.
  - **Cap the durable collection too.** Phase 10 capped the worker's slot payload at `MAX_RETAINED_DIAGNOSTICS` (`disk/constants.rs`), but this phase's partitioned-clearing rule never clears `DiagnosticKind::Disk`, so worker diagnostics would accumulate in `KeymapLoadFailures` for the life of the process — and re-append the truncation notice on every drain that discarded anything. Either cap `KeymapLoadFailures.diagnostics` with the same constant, or replace rather than append the worker's `Disk` entries. Bounding the mailbox without bounding the resource it feeds solves nothing.
  - **`DiskWorkerMessage.discarded_diagnostics` and `DiskWorkerChannels`' fields are `pub(super)` to `disk::worker`**, and `start_disk_worker_with` / `WorkerTimings` / `WatchMode` are too — so this phase can *read* a message but can never *construct* one, and cannot obtain channels except through the production path with a 100 ms debounce and a real filesystem watch. Its five acceptance cases all need synthetic input. **Therefore `commit_reload` takes `(Option<DiskSnapshot>, Vec<Diagnostic>)`, not a `DiskWorkerMessage`** — the plugin destructures the message at the call site, and the transaction is testable without touching `disk`.
  - **`DiskWorkerChannels` must not become a bevy `Resource` directly.** Nothing under `crates/hana_rubric/src/disk/` imports bevy today, and the dependency diagram above describes `disk` as depending only on `diagnostic`; a `#[derive(Resource)]` there breaks that. Wrap it in a `pub(crate)` newtype resource declared in `keymap_plugin.rs`. Two consequences to design around: the type is **not** `Clone`, and its `Drop` sends `Stop` and joins the worker thread (`disk/worker/channels.rs`), so app teardown blocks for at most one retry interval — **do not add a second shutdown system**; dropping the resource is the shutdown.
  - **The schema-generation `Err` arm needs a real answer.** "Records a diagnostic and continues without companion bytes" is not implementable as written: `start_disk_worker` takes `schema: Vec<u8>` with no skip form, and `publish_companion_files` would write a **zero-byte `keymap.schema.json` at `0444`**, breaking the `$schema` reference Phase 13's editor acceptance depends on and leaving a read-only file the next run cannot fix. Give the parameter an `Option<Vec<u8>>` form: on `Err`, start the worker with the defaults buffer and **skip schema publication entirely**.
- **Parse the header-prepended published buffer, not the raw embedded string.** This phase generates `keymap.default.jsonc` by prepending a JSONC comment header to the embedded default, and separately calls `from_sources(defaults_path, defaults_src, …)`. Those must be the **same bytes**: if `defaults_src` is `with_defaults`' raw `&'static str` while `defaults_path` names the published file, every `byte_range`, `line`, and `column` in a defaults-side diagnostic is off by the header's length and points at the wrong place in the file the user actually opens. The header is JSONC comments and Phase 6 tolerates them, so parsing the published buffer is both correct and free. Add to the acceptance gate: a defaults-side diagnostic's reported line number matches its true line in the published file.
- **Phase 7 already ships the whole parse-merge-compile pipeline; this phase calls it and does not rebuild it.** `MergedKeymap::from_sources(defaults_path, defaults_src, Option<(user_path, user_src)>, &CommandRegistry, &ConditionRegistry, protected_keystrokes: &[Keystroke]) -> Result<(Self, Vec<Diagnostic>), Vec<Diagnostic>>` (`crates/hana_rubric/src/keymap/merged.rs:90`) parses **both** sources, layers defaults under user edits, resolves tombstones, and validates commands, contexts, capabilities, and protected chords. `MergedKeymap::compile(&self, Generation, &CommandRegistry) -> CompiledKeymap` (`merged.rs:165`) builds the matchers. **`reload.rs` is therefore: UTF-8 decode → `from_sources` → `compile` → swap the resource.** Do not reimplement layering above the document stage.
- **D7's two tiers are `from_sources`'s `Result` arms — never the `DiagnosticKind`.** `Err` **is** the whole-file tier; it already folds in `KeymapDocument::parse`'s own `Err` for either source, and it carries every diagnostic collected before the failure as well as the failing document's own, so nothing is lost by matching at this level. `Ok((merged, diagnostics))` **is** the per-binding tier: every valid binding survives, every invalid one was skipped, and `diagnostics` reports them. **Both tiers emit `DiagnosticKind::Syntax`** (`crates/hana_rubric/src/keymap/document.rs:101` vs `:244`/`:516`), so classifying by `kind` ships the wrong behavior. `KeymapDocument::parse` itself is at `document.rs:35` if a lower-level entry point is ever needed, but this phase should not need it.
- **`Generation` has no successor function and no `Default`; this phase owns the counter.** `compiled.rs:21` is `pub(crate) struct Generation(pub(crate) usize)` — the field is `pub(crate)` deliberately, and nothing anywhere constructs or increments it. `Generation(previous.0 + 1)` is the intended construction; the first committed generation's number is this phase's choice.
- **Delete `keymap/mod.rs`'s `pub(crate) use merged::ResolvedEdits;` re-export and its `#[expect(unused_imports, …)]` attribute** (`crates/hana_rubric/src/keymap/mod.rs:13-17`) rather than adding accessors to satisfy it. `ResolvedEdits`'s `insert`/`global`/`for_condition` (`merged.rs:37`, `:49`, `:53`) are private to `merged.rs`, and this phase has no need for them — `from_sources` returns a finished `MergedKeymap`. The attribute must go in the same change either way: a fulfilled `expect` emits `unfulfilled_lint_expectation` under `-D warnings`.
- **`crates/hana_rubric/src/keymap/merged.rs:3-6` carries `#![expect(dead_code, reason = "keymap reload constructs merged keymaps after the loading transaction is added")]`.** This phase is that reader, so narrow or delete it here. Narrowing is often correct rather than deletion: a module-scope `expect` stays **fulfilled** while any single item in the file is still dead, so removing it wholesale can trade one lint error for another.
- **The byte-snapshot → `&str` conversion belongs to this phase, and invalid UTF-8 is a whole-file failure.** Phase 10 delivers bytes; `parse` takes `&str`; nothing between them owned the decode. A `str::from_utf8` failure produces one `DiagnosticKind::Syntax` diagnostic and leaves the last good keymap running — the same tier as a missing brace, since a partly-decodable file cannot be trusted to have delimited its bindings correctly.
- **`DiagnosticSeverity` is NOT the retained marker Phase 9's decision asks for. They are two separate fields and this phase must keep them separate.** Phase 7 shipped `pub severity: DiagnosticSeverity` with `Failure`/`Advisory` (`crates/hana_rubric/src/diagnostic.rs:71`), which answers "did this binding load?" — and `condition_diagnostic` writes `DiagnosticSeverity::Failure` (`crates/hana_rubric/src/condition.rs:222`). So under the retain-advisories/clear-failures rule alone, Phase 5's **build-time** context-registration failure is still deleted by the first clean load, which is precisely the bug Phase 9's decision exists to fix: it is written once at startup and nothing regenerates it. The retained collection answers a different question — "can any later load reproduce this?" — and `register_context`'s push site is what routes into it; the two `Diagnostic` construction sites in `condition.rs` keep `DiagnosticSeverity::Failure` unchanged.
- **An explicit `"context": null` is currently dropped with no feedback — add the missing advisory.** `MergedKeymap::resolve_context`'s `(None, Some(_))` arm (`crates/hana_rubric/src/keymap/merged.rs:248`) marks the block `Invalid` and pushes **no** diagnostic, while every other unresolvable-context path (`merged.rs:281`, `:300`) does push one. The block's bindings vanish silently. Emit an advisory `DiagnosticKind::Context` from that arm, stamped with the context source span `parse_context` already returns for the null case, saying a context must be a string naming a registered condition.
- **`Diagnostic::severity` is required at every construction site.** Phase 7 added it as a non-`Option` field on a struct with no `Default`, exactly like `message`. This phase's `Companion` and `Disk` diagnostics are `DiagnosticSeverity::Failure`.
- **Advisory diagnostics survive a successful load; only failure diagnostics are cleared by one.** Phase 7 added the advisory/failure severity distinction to `Diagnostic` and **already restated** `KeymapLoadFailures`'s doc contract as "Problems reported by the most recent keymap load" (`crates/hana_rubric/src/diagnostic.rs:79-89`) — that rewording is done, not pending. Advisories — an unrecognized block member, a duplicate context name, a duplicate binding key — arrive on the `Ok` path of a load that fully succeeded and must be **retained**, logged at warn level, so a later keymap-editing UI can list a user's mistakes and offer corrections. Combine this with the partitioned-clearing-plus-retained-collection rule this phase owns for Phase 5's build-time diagnostics: a successful reload clears only the *failure* diagnostics of the sources it regenerated.
- **`KeymapDocument::schema` is parsed and currently read by nobody.** `WireDocument.schema` / `KeymapDocument::schema` (`crates/hana_rubric/src/keymap/document.rs:28`, `WireDocument` at `:670-672`) hold the file's `"$schema"` value; Phase 9 *writes* that key into the generated default but no phase reads one back. Either give this phase a rule — warn when a user file's `$schema` does not point at the published `keymap.schema.json` — or state in the diff that the field is intentionally unread, so the next reader does not delete it as dead code.
- **The root object's member list has an editor-side authority and no load-time counterpart — add one.** Phase 8 enumerates the root's legal members (`$schema`, `bindings`) hand-written in `schema.rs` under `"additionalProperties": false`, while `WireDocument` carries no `deny_unknown_fields`, so an editor underlines an unknown root member that the loader silently ignores. Mirror the block-level treatment Phase 7 shipped: a shared constant of legal root members with an advisory `DiagnosticKind::Syntax` diagnostic when a user file carries one that is not in it, and the same schema-vs-loader agreement test `block_schema_members_match_the_loader_authority` already gives the block level. This is the root-level half of the user's standing rule that a mistake must be caught at load time for anyone editing without schema support, not only in the editor.
- Phase 9's companion publication is non-fatal and runs from the worker; its warnings are a distinct `DiagnosticKind`.
- **Phase 9's diagnostics-contention decision resolved 2026-07-30; this phase writes the rule.** `KeymapLoadFailures` gains a **second, separately-owned collection** holding diagnostics that no later load can regenerate — today that is exactly Phase 5's build-time context-registration failures, written once from `register_context` (`crates/hana_rubric/src/condition.rs:287-293`). The clearing performed on a successful keymap load **never touches that collection**, and within `diagnostics` it clears only *failure*-severity entries whose `kind` the keymap-load producer owns (`Syntax`, `Keystroke`, `Command`, `Context`), leaving advisories and the worker's `Companion`/`Disk` entries in place. With build-time failures moved out, `DiagnosticKind::Context` inside `diagnostics` is unambiguously reload-produced, which is what makes partitioning by `kind` sound. The change to already-shipped code is at the **push site**, not the construction site: `register_context` pushes into the retained collection instead of `diagnostics`. No field is added to `Diagnostic`, no `DiagnosticKind` variant is added, and Phase 5's resolved "reuse `DiagnosticKind::Context`" decision stands. Any consumer wanting every current problem reads both collections; expose that as one accessor on the resource rather than making callers remember.
- Phase 8's schema generator is a single `pub(crate)` entry point returning bytes; the plugin calls it and hands the bytes to the worker.
- **Phase 7's compile is pure with respect to `World` mutation, which is not the same as thread-free.** Both `from_sources` and `compile` take `&CommandRegistry` and `&ConditionRegistry`, and both are `Resource`s (`crates/hana_rubric/src/command/registry.rs:27`, `crates/hana_rubric/src/condition.rs:98`); `CompiledKeymap::from_merged` additionally clones every `CommandEntry` out of the registry (`crates/hana_rubric/src/keymap/compiled.rs:44-47`). So it runs in an ordinary `Res<…>` system and the swap is cheap — but nothing here licenses a task-pool build without first extracting both registries.
- Phase 4b's registry validation runs in `Plugin::finish`; the functions already exist and are called here for the first time in a real plugin. **`CommandRegistry::initialize` returns `Result<Self, Vec<Diagnostic>>` and does not insert itself into the world** — this phase owns that insertion. Reaching it needs `&mut World` (`app.world_mut()`), the same exclusive-access pattern Phase 11's `route_input` uses.

- **`Diagnostic::message` is required.** Phase 4a added `pub message: String` as a non-`Option` field on a struct with no `Default`, so every construction site in this phase must supply it. Use `String::new()` where no sentence applies.
- **This phase fulfils roughly ten dead-code expectations at once, not one — treat it as a sweep, not a checklist.** The whole worker surface is dead transitively today and goes live in a single diff, and every fulfilled `expect` is `unfulfilled_lint_expectation`, a hard error under `-D warnings`. **After wiring, run `rg -n 'expect\(dead_code|expect\(unused_imports' crates/hana_rubric/src/disk crates/hana_rubric/src/keymap` and narrow or delete every attribute this diff fulfils.** Known at the time of writing: the item-scoped one in `disk/worker/runtime.rs`; four `#[expect(unused_imports, …)]` on the re-exports in `disk/mod.rs`; five `#[cfg_attr(not(test), expect(dead_code, …))]` in `disk/worker/channels.rs` covering `DiskSnapshot`, `DiskWorkerChannels`, `take_message`, the shutdown path, and `WorkerControl::Stop`; plus the two already itemized above in `keymap/mod.rs` and `keymap/merged.rs`, and check `keymap/document.rs`. **Narrowing is often right rather than deletion** — a module-scope `expect` stays fulfilled while any single item in the file is still dead, so removing it wholesale can trade one lint error for another.
- **Test isolation is mandatory here and is not inherited — this phase is the first to start a worker from a Bevy `App`.** `Plugin::finish` starts the worker, so **any test that calls `app.finish()` — including this phase's own gate cases for duplicate-id rejection, an unresolvable shipped default, and `with_app_name` — writes a first-launch stub and companion files into the developer's real config directory.** Same rule as Phases 9 and 10: every test sets both the plugin's app-name parameter and `$XDG_CONFIG_HOME` to a temp directory and asserts the resolved path lies inside it before anything runs; **never unset `$XDG_CONFIG_HOME`, never consult the real home directory.** Phase 10's helpers are not reachable from here — `ENVIRONMENT_LOCK`, `TestDirectory`, and `XdgConfigHome` are `#[cfg(test)] pub(super)` in `crates/hana_rubric/src/disk/paths.rs`, visible only inside `crate::disk`. Widen all three to `pub(crate)`; they are already `#[cfg(test)]`, so this costs nothing in a release build. **Holding the shared `ENVIRONMENT_LOCK` is mandatory, not advisory:** unit tests share one binary and run in parallel, and Phase 9's and Phase 10's tests mutate the same process-wide variable — an unlocked test here corrupts theirs and theirs corrupts this one.

**Pending decision:**

**Decision A: add `.with_protected_keystroke(Keystroke)` to the builder**

Actual problem:
Phase 7 must reject any binding whose sequence starts with the protected recovery chord, and Phase 19 needs one canonical parsed constant for it. The value lives in hana's `global_shortcuts.rs`; `hana_rubric` may not depend on hana; and this plugin's builder — the only surface an application configures the keymap through — has no parameter for it. There is currently no route from the application into the library's validation.

What exists now:
- Phase 4a's `Unremappable` capability protects a *command*, not a keystroke: a user can still bind a different command to the recovery chord and shadow it.
- **The library half is already shipped.** Phase 7's `MergedKeymap::from_sources` and `from_documents` both take `protected_keystrokes: &[Keystroke]` and reject any sequence starting with one — bare chord or prefix — with a diagnostic saying it is reserved for the application's recovery command. Tests `merged.rs:1080`, `:1126`, `:1155` cover it. The "add a Phase 7 test" clause of this decision is satisfied.
- This phase's builder surface has no protected-keystroke parameter — that is the only remaining gap.
- The library deliberately never learns what an input context is, so it cannot infer the chord.

What should change:
- Add `.with_protected_keystroke(Keystroke)` to `KeymapPlugin`'s builder, accepting zero or more into a `Vec<Keystroke>` field on the plugin.
- Thread that field through to the existing `from_sources` parameter. No new validation logic — the enforcement already exists.
- Phase 19 supplies hana's `RECOVERY_CHORD` through this call.

Recommendation:
Take it. The application owns the value, the library already owns the enforcement, and the dependency direction stays intact. The remaining work is a builder field plus threading, and a test here that a protected keystroke registered through the builder reaches `from_sources`.

**Decision B: whether registry *and condition* validation stay all-or-nothing**

Actual problem:
Phase 4b's `validate_declarations` returns `Err` if **any** registered command fails, and `initialize` returns `Result<Self, Vec<Diagnostic>>` — so a single third-party crate shipping a command with an empty description leaves the application with *no* command registry at all, and therefore no keybindings whatsoever. This phase is where that `Err` is first handled in a real plugin, so this is where the policy is set.

**Phase 5 shipped the same all-or-nothing shape for conditions, which sharpens the question.** `ConditionRegistry::register` returns `Err` if any variant is bad and leaves the registry **entirely unchanged** (`crates/hana_rubric/src/condition.rs:150`); both context plugins then skip adding their sync system (`condition.rs:239-244`, `:265-270`). One hana context variant missing its `#[strum(message)]` therefore yields an *empty* condition registry — so every `"context"` in the shipped default keymap fails to resolve, and this phase's rule that `finish` "separately rejects any **shipped default** that does not resolve" turns a one-word omission into an app that does not start. Whatever policy this decision picks must cover both registries, or they will diverge.

What exists now:
- `crates/hana_rubric/src/command/registry.rs` — `validate_declarations` collects every diagnostic and returns `Err(Vec<Diagnostic>)` if the vector is non-empty; there is no partial-success path.
- `crates/hana_rubric/src/condition.rs` — `register` is atomic by construction: it builds a local `declarations` vector and only extends the registry after the diagnostics-empty early return.
- `clippy::panic` is denied workspace-wide, so the `Err` arm cannot abort.
- This phase already commits to two-tier failure for *keymap files* (D7 above): whole-file failure keeps the last good state, per-binding failure applies the good ones. Registry validation currently has no equivalent tier.

What should change — two routes:
1. **Keep all-or-nothing, degrade loudly.** `finish` records the diagnostics in `KeymapLoadFailures`, logs at error level, and inserts an **empty** `CommandRegistry`. Every keymap binding then fails to resolve and is individually reported, and the app starts. Simple, and a malformed declaration is a compile-time-adjacent programming error rather than user data. **Requires softening the shipped-default rule**: a shipped default that fails to resolve must not hard-fail startup when the registry is empty *because* validation already failed — otherwise a single bad declaration is fatal. Report it and start.
2. **Skip the bad command, keep the rest.** `initialize` gains a partial-success return — the valid entries plus the diagnostics — mirroring D7's per-binding tier. One bad third-party command costs only that command. Requires a Phase 4b signature change made here, and the matching change to `ConditionRegistry::register`.

Recommendation:
Route 1 for both registries, plus the shipped-default softening named above, plus a test that a context enum missing one `#[strum(message)]` yields a diagnostic and a **running** app rather than a startup failure. Command and condition declarations are code, not user-editable data, so the failure is a developer's to fix immediately; D7's per-item tier exists because keymap *files* are edited by users mid-session, which does not apply here. Route 2 also lets a partly-broken registry look healthy.

Approve this direction, or modify it?

**Acceptance gate:** `verify.sh check|test|lint hana_rubric` plus `verify.sh check fairy_dust` green — this phase edits `hana_rubric`'s crate root and prelude, and `fairy_dust` is the normal-dependency consumer where a broken re-export surfaces. **The five named acceptance cases each get a test:** reload while a key is held; remove or rebind that held action; context change during a pending sequence; parse failure during a pending sequence; multiple aliases held for the same action. Plus: `Plugin::finish` rejects a duplicate id and an unresolvable shipped default; a whole-file parse failure leaves the generation, pending sequence, and held actions untouched; a partial failure commits defaults plus every valid edit and reports the rest; the durable diagnostic resource is populated and readable after each. Plus, from the constraints above: **a clean keymap load leaves a startup context-registration diagnostic in place** (it is never regenerated, so clearing it loses it permanently); a value set through `with_app_name` survives the terminal `for_*_context` call and is observable after `build`; calling both context-source methods is rejected rather than silently double-registering; and a context enum missing one `#[strum(message)]` yields a diagnostic and a running app, not a startup failure. **Plus the generated reference header:** every registered condition appears in the comment block with its message text and the protected chord is named; the embedded default text that follows the header is byte-identical to the asset; and the whole file still parses as JSONC through `KeymapDocument::parse`.
