### Phase 13 — `hana_rubric`: editor acceptance · status: todo

#### Work Order

**Goal:** the schema actually works in a real editor with no workspace settings.

**Spec:**

**No benchmarks.** The input-path budget — no heap allocation, string parsing, reflection lookup, or full-keymap scan; inspect only current prefix candidates on a key edge; check the timeout only while a sequence is pending — is a *structural* property, and Phases 11 and 12 assert it directly with allocation counts. A wall-clock number nobody acts on is not worth a bench target, a `criterion` dev-dependency, or the `bench_support` feature that would be needed to reach the crate-private routing types. Phase 3 therefore adds no `[features]` section.

**Editor acceptance (manual, recorded in the phase retrospective).** Fresh profile in **both Zed and VS Code**:
- the relative `"$schema": "./keymap.schema.json"` resolves,
- completions work with **no workspace settings**,
- comments produce **no diagnostics** in the `.jsonc` file.

The manual pass no longer covers "a repeat launch does not overwrite the user stub" — Phase 10 shipped that as an automated test (`stub_is_created_once_and_never_overwritten`, `disk/worker/runtime.rs`, built on `OpenOptions::create_new(true)`, and covering the zero-byte-existing-file case too). Only the three editor-dependent bullets above need a human.

Build a tiny example app in `crates/hana_rubric/examples/` that registers a handful of commands and one context enum, runs once to publish the companion files, and gives the manual check something real to point an editor at. The example doubles as the crate's documentation.

**Description coverage.** Assert the full-coverage test from Phase 8 against the example's command and condition set: **the test fails when any *user-bindable* public command or any condition lacks a description or is missing from generated completion.** `Unremappable` commands are excluded by design — Phase 8 does not emit them, because Phase 7 rejects a file that names one.

**Files:**
- `crates/hana_rubric/examples/` — create the demonstration app
- `crates/hana_rubric/Cargo.toml` — example target and dev-dependencies

**Constraints from prior phases:**
- Phase 12 delivered the full pipeline behind `KeymapPlugin`; the example wires exactly the public builder surface a downstream app would.
- Phase 9's companion publication writes `keymap.default.jsonc` and `keymap.schema.json` at `0444` under `$XDG_CONFIG_HOME` (or `~/.config/<app>`); the app name is a plugin parameter, so the example uses its own.
- Phase 10's first-launch stub is created only if absent and never overwritten, and Phase 10 already tests it — this phase does not re-test it.
- **A read-back test needs no sleep and is not flaky.** Phase 10's worker publishes the companion files *before* entering its watch loop, and `Drop for DiskWorkerChannels` sends `Stop` and joins the worker thread (`crates/hana_rubric/src/disk/worker/channels.rs`). So by the time the example app has run and dropped its plugin resources, `keymap.default.jsonc` and `keymap.schema.json` are on disk and complete. Do **not** add a sleep, a retry loop, or a polling wait, and do not weaken the assertion on suspicion of flakiness — if a read-back fails, the publication genuinely failed.
- **Gate commands: `verify.sh example hana_rubric <name>` belongs to this phase only**, because this is the phase whose Files own the example.

- **A `command!` call site needs five names imported unqualified**, not four: `InputAction`, `Event`, `Reflect`, `ReflectEvent`, and `ReflectKeymapCommand`. The expansion writes `#[reflect(Event, KeymapCommand)]`, so bevy's derive forms *both* `ReflectEvent` and `ReflectKeymapCommand` by concatenation in the caller's scope. **`hana_rubric::prelude::*` does not cover this** — the prelude re-exports types only — 25 of them since Phase 5 added the condition surface and Phase 7 added `DiagnosticSeverity` on top of Phase 4c's keystroke surface (`ActiveCondition`, `Capability`, `CommandId`, `CommandInfo`, `CommandRegistry`, `ConditionName`, `Diagnostic`, `DiagnosticKind`, `HoldPhase`, `Keybindings`, `KeymapCommand`, `KeymapContext`, `KeymapLoadFailures`, `KeymapPlugin`, `KeymapSystems`, `Keystroke`, `KeystrokeSequence`, `MatchOutcome`, `Modifiers`, `ReflectKeymapCommand`, `SequenceMatcher`, and the three keystroke error types) — but **not** the `command!` macro and **not** `Event`/`Reflect`/`ReflectEvent`/`InputAction`. Write the five imports explicitly.
- **`command!`'s keys are positional and fixed**: `action`, `event`, `id`, `title`, `description`, and optionally `capability` — in that order, `capability` last. It is `macro_rules!` arm matching, not a keyword parser; a key out of order produces an opaque "no rules expected this token" error. Omitting `capability` yields `Capability::OneShot`. **`capability: Held` is a deliberate `compile_error!`** — a held command is declared with the leading `held,` marker and no `capability:` key, which is what gives its event the `phase: HoldPhase` field. `capability: Capability::Held` matches no arm at all (the generic arm takes a bare ident).
- **Every `KeymapCommand` impl must satisfy `Event + Reflect` and implement `fn hold_phase(&self) -> Option<HoldPhase>`** (`crates/hana_rubric/src/command/reflect_command.rs:15`). `command!` generates the method — non-held forms return `None`, the `held,` form returns `Some(self.phase)`. A hand-written impl that omits it fails to compile; that is deliberate, so a held command cannot register as silently inert.
**Pending decision:**

**Decision: how Phase 13 re-asserts description coverage against the example**

Actual problem:
This phase asks to "assert the full-coverage test from Phase 8 against the example's command and condition set", but Phase 8's `schema_bytes` is `pub(crate)` and everything in `crates/hana_rubric/examples/` compiles against the crate's **public** API only. There is no way for example code, or a test that instantiates the example's registries, to reach the generator. Phase 8 also already ships `every_user_bindable_command_and_condition_has_a_described_completion`, which is generic over whichever registries it is handed — so the requirement as written is both unimplementable and partly redundant.

What exists now:
- `crates/hana_rubric/src/keymap/schema.rs` — `pub(crate) fn schema_bytes(…)`, plus a coverage test that builds its own registries.
- This phase's Files: `crates/hana_rubric/examples/` and `crates/hana_rubric/Cargo.toml` only.
- Phase 9 publishes `keymap.schema.json` to disk at `0444` under the app's config directory.

What should change — two routes:
1. **Assert against the published file** (recommended). An integration test in `crates/hana_rubric/tests/` runs the example app once with `$XDG_CONFIG_HOME` pointed at a temp directory, reads the `keymap.schema.json` it wrote, and asserts every command the example registers appears with a non-empty description and every condition appears with its message. This tests the whole chain — generate, publish, read back — which is what this phase is actually for, and needs no visibility change. Adds `crates/hana_rubric/tests/` to this phase's Files.
2. **Move the example's declarations into a crate-internal module** so Phase 8's existing test can instantiate them directly, with the example importing them. Cheaper to write, but it tests the generator again rather than the published artifact, and it puts example-only code in `src/`.

Recommendation:
Route 1. The generator is already covered by Phase 8's own test; what is unproven is that the bytes survive publication and land where an editor will find them, and only an end-to-end read-back shows that. Add `crates/hana_rubric/tests/` to this phase's Files and replace the "assert the full-coverage test from Phase 8 against the example's command and condition set" clause with the read-back assertion.

Approve this direction, or modify it?

**Real schema validation belongs here, and nowhere earlier.** Phase 8 could not validate its output against a real JSON Schema validator — no such crate is in the dependency set and that phase forbade a manifest change — so its tests use a hand-written partial validator and the gap was recorded rather than closed. This phase's **Files** already own `crates/hana_rubric/Cargo.toml`'s dev-dependencies, so add a validator crate there and a test that the generated schema is a valid draft-07 document and that a keymap with a misspelled root or block member fails validation under it.

**Acceptance gate:** `verify.sh check|test|lint hana_rubric` green plus `verify.sh example hana_rubric <name>`. The manual editor check is performed in both Zed and VS Code on a fresh profile and its result recorded. The description-coverage test passes. The generated schema validates under a real JSON Schema validator, and a document with a misspelled member fails under it.

### Phase 14 — hana: `InteractionContext` · status: todo

**Blocked by:** nothing — `G1` is superseded. Work on `init/hana_catalyst` in `/Users/natemccoy/rust/hana_tool_graph`; reconcile **Files** and **Spec** against that branch before dispatch.

#### Work Order

**Goal:** the context enum the whole condition system depends on exists in code for the first time.

**Spec:**

**It does not exist anywhere today.** `hana/docs/hana/computed_state.md` is committed architecture, but the enum lives **only in that document** — no code in the tree references it. D3's condition names, load-time validation, schema generation, and per-context compiled matchers all depend on it.

Create `hana/crates/hana/src/input/interaction_context.rs` holding **`InteractionContext` and its computed-state derivation only.** Hana writes **no sync system** — R7 deleted that boilerplate. It derives the strum traits and calls `for_state_context`.

```rust
#[derive(States, Copy, Clone, Eq, PartialEq, Hash, Debug, EnumIter, AsRefStr, EnumMessage)]
#[strum(serialize_all = "snake_case")]
enum InteractionContext {
    #[strum(message = "…")]
    Variant,
    // …
}
```

- Variants and their derivation come from `docs/hana/computed_state.md` — read it and follow it; it is committed architecture, not a suggestion.
- **Every variant needs `#[strum(message = "…")]`.** A missing one is caught by startup validation, not the compiler.
- The enum shape carries D3's mutual-exclusivity guarantee **structurally** — one variant is active by construction, not by assumption.
- **It needs no Bevy reflection.** `EnumInfo::variant_names()` is not used and `reflect_documentation` is not needed for condition descriptions.
- Hana takes the **state** path (R8): `computed_state.md` derives the context from selection, lock engagement, and modal presence, which is what Bevy's computed states are for.

**This is the one hana-side phase with no collision against `init/hana_catalyst`** — it is an entirely new file. If catalyst stalls, it may be pulled forward on its own branch; nothing else here can be.

**Files:**
- `hana/crates/hana/src/input/interaction_context.rs` — create
- `hana/crates/hana/src/input/mod.rs` — declare the module
- `hana/Cargo.toml` — `strum` is already a workspace dep at 0.28.0; confirm the derive features

**Constraints from prior phases:**
- Phase 5 defined `KeymapContext` as a **blanket impl** — hana writes no impl, only derives. Decision A resolved as recommended, so the shipped bound list is `AsRef<str> + Copy + EnumMessage + Eq + IntoEnumIterator + Send + Sync + 'static` (`crates/hana_rubric/src/condition.rs:51-59`), with **no `Resource` bound**; `for_state_context::<C>()` adds `C: States` itself and `for_context::<C>()` adds `C: Resource`. Two things to read carefully: the bound is `AsRef<str>` (the std trait) while the *derive* that satisfies it is strum's `AsRefStr` — different names for the two halves — and `Send + Sync` are new relative to the earlier draft. Phase 5's test `derived_state_context_satisfies_keymap_context_without_a_resource_impl` proves a `States`-only enum satisfies the trait, which is exactly hana's case here.
- **A computed state that can be absent used to panic the sync system; Phase 11 fixed it, and this phase should assert the fix rather than work around it.** `bevy_state 0.19.0` removes `State<S>` from the world whenever `compute` returns `None`, and Phase 5 shipped `sync_state_condition` taking `Res<State<C>>` unconditionally — a hard panic on any such frame. Phase 11 relaxes it to `Option<Res<State<C>>>`, so `InteractionContext::compute` does **not** have to be total; a frame with no applicable variant simply leaves `ActiveCondition` at its last value. If this phase runs before that relaxation lands, it must make it itself.
- Phase 5's `for_state_context::<C>()` reads `Res<State<C>>`; wiring the call is Phase 17, not this phase.
- Phase 5 rejects duplicate condition names at load and diagnoses a missing `#[strum(message)]` at startup.

**Acceptance gate:** `verify.sh check|test|lint hana` green. Tests prove: the enum satisfies the strum bounds `KeymapContext` requires (compile-time assertion against the trait); every variant carries a non-empty message; the computed-state derivation produces the variants `computed_state.md` specifies for the selection/lock/modal combinations it names.

### Phase 15 — hana: dependency unification and `reflect_auto_register` · status: todo

**Blocked by:** nothing — `G1` is superseded. Work on `init/hana_catalyst` in `/Users/natemccoy/rust/hana_tool_graph`; reconcile **Files** and **Spec** against that branch before dispatch. Clears `G2` on completion.

#### Work Order

**Goal:** one **`hana_rubric`** instance in hana's dependency graph, `hana_rubric` reachable, and reflect auto-registration on. (`hana_rubric` is the load-bearing one — it now owns every type that crosses the boundary. A duplicate `bevy_kana` is hygiene, not correctness.)

**Spec:**

**The problem this fixes — substantially smaller after Phase 4c.** As originally written, this phase's core hazard was type identity: hana consumed `bevy_kana` from crates.io while `hana_rubric` consumed the workspace copy, and hana's `Cargo.lock` on `main` already resolved **two** `bevy_kana` instances — one direct from crates.io, one git-rev pulled transitively via `bevy_diegetic`, `hana_conduit`, and `hana_prosody`. With `Keystroke` and `SequenceMatcher` living in `bevy_kana`, two instances meant two distinct types across the boundary and nothing type-checked.

**Phase 4c moved those types into `hana_rubric`, so that hazard is gone.** hana pins `hana_rubric` by git rev and gets one definition of `Keystroke`, `KeystrokeSequence`, `SequenceMatcher`, and `MatchOutcome` regardless of how many `bevy_kana` instances the graph contains. What remains for this phase is ordinary: add the `hana_rubric` git-rev pin, drop hana's now-invalid `features = ["input"]` on `bevy_kana`, repoint the macro imports (below), and turn on `reflect_auto_register`.

**Still verify hana's actual graph before dispatching.** `bevy_kana` may or may not still be a direct hana dependency after this — it depends on whether anything in hana uses `cascade` or `math`. If it is, duplicate instances are now a hygiene problem rather than a correctness one, but collapsing them is still the right call. **Do not trust any version number quoted in this plan** — Phase 1 published `0.2.0`, `main` has since released `0.3.0`, and the workspace member is `0.4.0-dev`. Re-read hana's `Cargo.toml` and `Cargo.lock` at dispatch time.

**Decision: git-rev pin, not publish** (G2), following the `hana_prosody` precedent.

**Pinning `hana_rubric` alone IS sufficient for type identity.** `hana_rubric` has no `bevy_kana` dependency at all after Phase 4c (`crates/hana_rubric/Cargo.toml`), so `Keystroke` identity does not depend on `bevy_kana`'s rev. If hana keeps a `bevy_kana` dependency, moving it to the same git rev is still worth doing for hygiene and to drop the now-invalid `features = ["input"]`, but it is no longer a correctness requirement and does not have to share the commit.

- Add `hana_rubric = { git = "…", rev = "<rev>" }` and change `bevy_kana` to `{ git = "…", rev = "<same rev>" }` — **no `features = ["input"]`**, because Phase 4c deleted that feature.
- `init/hana_catalyst` has already moved every git dep to rev `df97b2b`, replaced `bevy_diegetic` with `hana_diegetic`, and added `hana_lading`. **Verify against the landed state** — the rev to pin is whatever `bevy_hana`'s `feature/rubric` merges to, and every other `bevy_hana`-sourced dep must be on that same rev.
- After the change, `cargo tree -p hana -i bevy_kana -e normal` must show **exactly one** instance. `-e normal` is mandatory: Phase 1 hit a dev-dependency phantom where the default edge set reported a crate present through a dev-only cycle, and the same trap applies here.
- The ambiguous-package-spec workaround Phase 1 established was verified against **this** workspace only. hana is a different workspace with different members — re-verify that `-p hana` and `-p bevy_kana` resolve unambiguously there once the pin lands, before relying on either line as a gate.

**`reflect_auto_register`** — enable it on hana's `bevy` dependency. It is already enabled in `bevy_hana`; this brings hana in line (D4 amendment). **Note M4: automatic registration never covers generic instantiations**, so any `Action<A>`-shaped or otherwise generic command type still needs an explicit `register_type`.

**`reflect_documentation` is not needed and must not be added.** R6 authors command text through `command!`; R7 puts condition descriptions on the enum variants. Adding it would cost binary size for embedded doc strings and buy nothing.

- **Repoint every macro import in the same commit as the pin.** Phase 4c moved `event!`, `action!`, `bind_action_system!`, and `Keybindings` out of `bevy_kana` and into `hana_rubric`, so hana's ~60 `use bevy_kana::{event, action, bind_action_system, Keybindings}` sites must become `hana_rubric::…`. This is a mechanical import swap with **no semantic change** — do not migrate any declaration to `command!` here, that is Phase 16's job. Splitting the swap out of this commit leaves hana uncompilable for the whole gap between Phases 15 and 16.
- `bevy_kana`'s dependency entry also loses `features = ["input"]`, because Phase 4c deleted the feature.

**Files:**
- `hana/Cargo.toml` — the `bevy_kana` and `hana_rubric` dependency entries, `bevy`'s feature list
- `hana/crates/hana/Cargo.toml` — member-level dependency entries if they exist there
- `hana/crates/hana/src/**` — the ~60 macro import sites: `input/global_shortcuts.rs` (32 `action!`), the 28 files declaring an `event!` next to its handler, and the ~18 `bind_action_system!` sites (`selection/operations.rs`, `selection/dimension_lock/mod.rs`, `persistence.rs`, `selection/rotate/mod.rs`, `voice/mod.rs`, `camera/editor_camera.rs`, `camera/flyover.rs`, `transport.rs`, `input/escape.rs`)
- `hana/Cargo.lock` — regenerated; commit it

**Constraints from prior phases:**
- Phases 1–13 shipped the `hana_rubric` crate on `bevy_hana`'s `feature/rubric` branch. The rev to pin is that branch's merge commit. **Phase 4c moved the `keystroke` module (and the rest of `input/`) out of `bevy_kana` into `hana_rubric`**, so `Keystroke`, `KeystrokeSequence`, `SequenceMatcher`, and `MatchOutcome` are `hana_rubric` types, ungated, and `bevy_kana` no longer has an `input` feature at all.
- **The rev to pin must be computed after every phase that changes `hana_rubric`'s public surface.** The old "Phases 4a, 4c, and 11 are the only ones permitted to edit `bevy_kana`" rule is retired — Phase 4c left `bevy_kana` with no input surface, and Phase 11's option B is now a `hana_rubric` edit. What still matters is that Phases 4a–13 all shape `hana_rubric`, so a change landing after the rev is chosen means hana pins a tree missing it and the mismatch surfaces as a type error in hana rather than in this workspace. Confirm the branch tip before reading `git rev-parse`.
- **`.claude/config/release.toml` needs no change for this phase.** Its single `[[publish_path_pins]]` entry (`bevy_kana = "0.3.0"`) exists because `bevy_lagrange` takes `bevy_kana` as a *normal* dependency. Phase 4c added `hana_rubric` as a **dev**-dependency of `bevy_lagrange` and `hana_valence` and a normal dependency of `fairy_dust` — cargo strips path-only dev-deps on publish, and `fairy_dust` is not published (workspace version `0.0.1`, no pin entry, itself only a path-only dev-dep of `bevy_lagrange`). A `hana_rubric` pin becomes necessary only if `hana_rubric` is published or `fairy_dust` becomes publishable.
- `bevy_kana` has **no `input` feature** by the time this phase runs — Phase 4c deleted it and moved its contents to `hana_rubric`. Any `features = ["input"]` left on a `bevy_kana` entry is now an error, not a requirement. (Before 4c the feature existed but was outside the default set, `default = ["math"]`, despite `lib.rs`'s doc comment claiming otherwise.)

**Pending decision:**

**Decision: re-affirm "pin, not publish" before this phase dispatches (G2)**

Actual problem:
This phase's `G2` rationale — pin `bevy_kana` and `hana_rubric` by git rev rather than publishing them — was reasoned at a time when publishing `bevy_kana` had never been done and was treated as the risky path. Phase 1 has since published `bevy_kana 0.2.0` to crates.io successfully, so the precedent that argued against publishing no longer holds on its own terms.

What exists now:
- `bevy_kana 0.2.0` and `0.3.0` are both on crates.io; this workspace's member is `version = "0.4.0-dev"`.
- `hana_rubric` has never been published and is a new crate on `feature/rubric`.
- hana's dependency graph has not been re-read since Phase 1; the version numbers quoted in this phase's Spec are known stale.

What should change:
- Re-read hana's `Cargo.toml` and `Cargo.lock` and re-run `cargo tree -p hana -i bevy_kana -e normal` immediately before dispatching, then decide between:
  - **Pin by git rev** (current plan): one commit moves both deps, nothing is published, and the rev can be moved freely while the plan is still in flight.
  - **Publish**: `bevy_kana 0.4.0` (breaking — 4c removed `input`) plus a first `hana_rubric` release, and hana consumes both by version. Cleaner for hana, but it locks the API of a crate the remaining phases are still changing. Publishing `hana_rubric` would also require a `[[publish_path_pins]]` entry in `.claude/config/release.toml`, which today holds only `bevy_kana = "0.3.0"`.

Recommendation:
Keep the git-rev pin. Phases 4a and 11 may still edit `bevy_kana` and Phases 4a–13 all shape `hana_rubric`'s public surface, so publishing now means republishing after nearly every remaining phase. Publishing becomes the right move once the plan is exhausted, not here. The re-verification of hana's actual graph is still required regardless of which way this resolves.




**Acceptance gate:** `verify.sh check|test|lint hana` green. `cargo tree -p hana -i hana_rubric -e normal` shows exactly one instance. A trivial test in hana constructs a `hana_rubric::Keystroke` and passes it to a `hana_rubric` API, proving the pin resolves and the type is reachable. `rg 'bevy_kana::(event|action|bind_action_system|Keybindings|Keystroke|KeystrokeSequence|SequenceMatcher|MatchOutcome)' hana/crates/hana/src` returns nothing — every one of those names moved to `hana_rubric` in Phase 4c, and hana compiles without a single declaration having changed shape. If `bevy_kana` survives as a hana dependency at all (only if something there uses `cascade` or `math`), `cargo tree -p hana -i bevy_kana -e normal` should also show exactly one instance; if nothing uses it, the entry is removed rather than deduplicated.

### Phase 16 — hana: migrate 32 declarations to `command!` and relocate them · status: todo

**Blocked by:** G2. (`G1` superseded — work on `init/hana_catalyst` in `/Users/natemccoy/rust/hana_tool_graph`, reconcile before dispatch.)

#### Work Order

**Goal:** every command is declared with its id, authored title, description, and capability, next to the handler that implements it.

**Spec:**

All **32** `action!` declarations in `crates/hana/src/input/global_shortcuts.rs` (lines 6–41) switch to `command!` and **gain authored text — none carries a doc comment today.** The count is 32, not 35: `input/mod.rs` has 33 `pub use` lines and the extra is `GlobalShortcutsContext`, not an action. One of the 32 (`ModifySelection`) is a held modifier, and `hana_rubric` adds three private modifier action types on top (`PrimaryShortcutsModifier`, `AltModifier`, `ControlModifier` in `crates/hana_rubric/src/command/keybindings.rs`).

**Each invocation goes where its handler already is.** Leaving all 32 in `global_shortcuts.rs` would make that file a cross-domain data dictionary — camera, persistence, selection, transport, voice, tools, inspectors — satisfying several split criteria at once and separating every command type from its primary behavior.

Placement: `camera/editor_camera.rs` (`CameraHomeEvent` already lives there), `camera/flyover.rs`, `selection/operations.rs` (delete, duplicate, select-all, zoom), `selection/dimension_lock/`, `selection/rotate/`, `persistence.rs`, `transport.rs`, `voice/mod.rs`, `tool/light_block.rs`, `tool/metal_sphere.rs`, `input/escape.rs`, and the existing camera/light/selection/inspector setting modules for their switch commands.

**The command re-exports come out of `input/mod.rs`.** The generated types stay `pub` — `action!` and `event!` hardcode `pub struct $name;` and `command!` adds no visibility parameter (settled Phase 4a Decision C). `pub` inside a binary crate leaks nothing.

**Ids** follow `subsystem::verb_object` in snake_case, namespaced per the comment groupings already in `global_shortcuts.rs`: `camera`, `selection`, `dimension_lock`, `transport`, `scene`, `voice`, `spawn`, `inspect`. **These ids ship in user keymap files and outlive most other choices here** — there is no alias machinery in v1 (A1), so a rename later is a breaking change for users. Choose carefully.

**Capabilities:** `ModifySelection` and `FreezeTracking` are `Held` (Phase 18 rewires them). Commands belonging to `PermanentShortcutsContext` are `Unremappable` (Phase 19). Everything else is `OneShot`.

**Scale — larger than the source breakdown implies.** 20 files import from `crate::input`; dispatch is **23 `bind_action_system!` call sites across 10 files plus 10 `bind_action_switch!` across 8 more**. All are candidate edits once the re-exports leave `input/mod.rs`.

**This phase does not change behavior.** The static `action!` → `Start<A>` → `bind_action_system!` → semantic event chain stays exactly as it is; `command!` wraps the same two macros and adds metadata. Bindings still come from `global_shortcuts.rs`'s `spawn_*` calls until Phase 17.

**Files:**
- `hana/crates/hana/src/input/global_shortcuts.rs` — remove the 32 declarations
- `hana/crates/hana/src/input/mod.rs` — remove the 32 command re-exports (keep `GlobalShortcutsContext`)
- The ~12 handler modules listed above — add `command!` invocations with authored text
- The ~20 files importing from `crate::input` — update import paths
- The 23 `bind_action_system!` and 10 `bind_action_switch!` call sites — update paths

**Constraints from prior phases:**
- Phase 4a's `command!` takes **both identifiers** — stable `macro_rules!` cannot derive `CameraHomeEvent` from `CameraHome`: `command! { action: X, event: XEvent, id: "…", title: "…", description: "…" }`.
- Phase 4a: **`ReflectKeymapCommand` must be imported unqualified at every call site** — bevy's reflect derive resolves `#[reflect(KeymapCommand)]` through an unqualified name in the invocation module. It is not the only one: `command!` wraps `action!` and `event!`, which expand to bare `#[derive(InputAction)]` and `#[derive(Event, Reflect)]` with **no internal imports**, so `InputAction`, `Event`, and `Reflect` must also resolve unqualified. Every one of the 32 call sites needs:
  ```rust
  use bevy::prelude::{Event, Reflect};
  use bevy_enhanced_input::prelude::InputAction;
  use hana_rubric::{command, ReflectKeymapCommand};
  ```
- Phase 4a settled Decision C: generated command types stay `pub`. Do not attempt `pub(crate)`; the wrapped macros have no visibility parameter.
- Phase 4a: `id` is const-validated at expansion (exactly one `::`, snake_case segments); title and description are required and rejected empty by startup validation.
- Phase 15 pinned `hana_rubric` to one instance and enabled `reflect_auto_register`, so `#[reflect(KeymapCommand)]` types register automatically — **except generics (M4)**.

- **A `command!` call site needs five names imported unqualified**, not four: `InputAction`, `Event`, `Reflect`, `ReflectEvent`, and `ReflectKeymapCommand`. The expansion writes `#[reflect(Event, KeymapCommand)]`, so bevy's derive forms *both* `ReflectEvent` and `ReflectKeymapCommand` by concatenation in the caller's scope. **`hana_rubric::prelude::*` does not cover this** — the prelude re-exports types only — 25 of them since Phase 5 added the condition surface and Phase 7 added `DiagnosticSeverity` on top of Phase 4c's keystroke surface (`ActiveCondition`, `Capability`, `CommandId`, `CommandInfo`, `CommandRegistry`, `ConditionName`, `Diagnostic`, `DiagnosticKind`, `HoldPhase`, `Keybindings`, `KeymapCommand`, `KeymapContext`, `KeymapLoadFailures`, `KeymapPlugin`, `KeymapSystems`, `Keystroke`, `KeystrokeSequence`, `MatchOutcome`, `Modifiers`, `ReflectKeymapCommand`, `SequenceMatcher`, and the three keystroke error types) — but **not** the `command!` macro and **not** `Event`/`Reflect`/`ReflectEvent`/`InputAction`. Write the five imports explicitly.
- **`command!`'s keys are positional and fixed**: `action`, `event`, `id`, `title`, `description`, and optionally `capability` — in that order, `capability` last. It is `macro_rules!` arm matching, not a keyword parser; a key out of order produces an opaque "no rules expected this token" error. Omitting `capability` yields `Capability::OneShot`. **`capability: Held` is a deliberate `compile_error!`** — a held command is declared with the leading `held,` marker and no `capability:` key, which is what gives its event the `phase: HoldPhase` field. `capability: Capability::Held` matches no arm at all (the generic arm takes a bare ident).
- **Every `KeymapCommand` impl must satisfy `Event + Reflect` and implement `fn hold_phase(&self) -> Option<HoldPhase>`** (`crates/hana_rubric/src/command/reflect_command.rs:15`). `command!` generates the method — non-held forms return `None`, the `held,` form returns `Some(self.phase)`. A hand-written impl that omits it fails to compile; that is deliberate, so a held command cannot register as silently inert.
**Pending decision:** `ModifySelection` and `FreezeTracking` have no semantic event, but `command!` mandates one

Actual problem:
This phase migrates 32 declarations to `command!`, but hana declares only 30 `event!` types against 32 `action!` declarations. `ModifySelection` and `FreezeTracking` are consumed as `Action<A>` state (`hana/crates/hana/src/selection/click_select.rs:34`, `box_select/drag.rs:54`) and as `Start`/`Complete` observers (`selection/dimension_lock/position.rs:42,50`) — neither has a semantic event, and neither is among the 30. Phase 4a implemented `KeymapCommand` on the **event** (`crates/hana_rubric/src/command/reflect_command.rs:114`), so `command!` cannot declare a command without one.

What exists now:
- `command!` has four public arms; every one that declares a command requires `event:` — there is no event-only or action-only form.
- `KeymapCommand: Event + Reflect` is a **supertrait** (`crates/hana_rubric/src/command/reflect_command.rs:15`), so the metadata carrier must itself be an event.
- This phase's Spec says it does not change behavior, and its Files list does not account for inventing new types.

What should change (three options):
1. This phase declares `ModifySelectionEvent` and `FreezeTrackingEvent` via `command! { held, … }`. They are **not** dead types: Phase 4b's `register_held_observer` observes each one and writes its `CustomInput` on `HoldPhase::Begin` / `End`, so the event is the scripted, BRP, and test path into a hold that no keyboard is pressing. Nothing else needs to observe them.
2. ~~Add an action-only arm and move `KeymapCommand` off the event onto a separate carrier~~ — **ruled out by shipped code.** `KeymapCommand: Event + Reflect`; a non-event carrier does not compile.
3. Leave both commands out of the migration entirely; they stay raw `action!` declarations and are not remappable — but then Phase 18 has no `CustomInput` to bind and cannot proceed.

Recommendation:
Option 1. The two events are the only way BRP, a script, or a test can drive those two holds, so they carry real behavior rather than being inert metadata. This phase's Spec and Files list need updating to say so; as written it claims no new types.

Approve this direction, or modify it?

- **`command!` has a third public arm for held commands**, marked by a leading `held` token and carrying no `capability:` key: `command! { held, action: …, event: …, id: …, title: …, description: … }`. Its generated event is `pub struct $Event { pub phase: HoldPhase }`, not a unit struct. Use this form for every command migrated as `Held`.
- **`Held` commands are absent from the command palette** (Phase 4b, Decision E). They still need an authored title and description — those are the key-binding editor's label and the schema's hover text.

**Acceptance gate:** `verify.sh check|test|lint hana` green. All 32 commands are declared with non-empty titles and descriptions and resolve through the registry at startup with no duplicate-id rejection. `rg 'action!\(' crates/hana/src/input/global_shortcuts.rs` returns nothing. Every existing keyboard shortcut still works — behavior is unchanged this phase.

### Phase 17 — hana: `global_shortcuts.rs` becomes the default keymap · status: todo

**Blocked by:** G2. (`G1` superseded — work on `init/hana_catalyst` in `/Users/natemccoy/rust/hana_tool_graph`, reconcile before dispatch.)

#### Work Order

**Goal:** the bindings live in a JSONC file instead of Rust, and the keymap plugin is wired with hana's context enum.

**Spec:**

**`global_shortcuts.rs` stops being the keymap.** Its hardcoded `spawn_key` / `spawn_shift_key` / `spawn_platform_key` / `spawn_binding` calls (`spawn_actions`, `spawn_switches`, `spawn_commands`, `spawn_platform_commands`, lines 46–108) move into the embedded default `.jsonc` that ships via `include_str!`.

What remains in Rust: `GlobalShortcutsContext`, `PermanentShortcutsContext` (Phase 19), and the two held actions' entity wiring (Phase 18).

**Translate each existing binding faithfully** — this is a behavior-preserving migration:
- `spawn_key::<A>()` → the bare key string (it blocks all modifiers today; the matcher's canonical `Keystroke` with an empty modifier set is the equivalent).
- `spawn_shift_key::<A>()` → `"shift-<key>"`.
- `spawn_platform_key::<A>()` → `"cmd-<key>"`, which the parser folds into control off macOS inside `Modifiers::with_platform()`. (`PlatformShortcutMode` lives at `crates/hana_rubric/src/platform_shortcut_mode.rs` as `pub(crate)` after Phase 4c — hana cannot see it.)
- `spawn_binding::<A, B>()` → whatever chord the bundle expresses.

**Defaults are embedded and always load from the embedded copy, never from disk** — they must load with no config directory at all, the same constraint the recovery palette carries in `tool-graph.md`.

The default file carries **explanatory comments** (a use for choosing JSONC) including the documentation of the unremappable recovery chord required by D8.

**Wire the plugin:**

```rust
app.add_plugins(
    KeymapPlugin::new()
        .with_app_name("hana")
        .with_defaults(include_str!("…/keymap.default.jsonc"))
        .for_state_context::<InteractionContext>(),
);
```

**The `Escape` chain — decide here.** D3 deliberately left this open: the chain is an **ordered fallthrough, not mutual exclusion**. `escape.rs` tries `EscapeStep::{CloseOutputWindow, CloseInspectors, ClearSelection}` in order, first `EscapeHandled::Yes` wins. Whether it becomes ordered conditions or keeps today's consumption-based chain **is decided by this phase**. Note that `init/hana_catalyst` also changed `escape.rs` (+18/−2) and made it call `hana_catalyst::Graph::disconnect`, pulling a `hana_catalyst` dependency into `input/` — reconcile against the landed version before choosing. **Record the decision in the phase retrospective either way.**

**Files:**
- `hana/crates/hana/src/input/global_shortcuts.rs` — delete the four `spawn_*` functions; keep the contexts
- `hana/crates/hana/assets/` or a suitable in-tree location — create `keymap.default.jsonc`
- `hana/crates/hana/src/input/mod.rs` — add `KeymapPlugin` to `EnhancedInputAppPlugin`
- `hana/crates/hana/src/input/escape.rs` — per the decision above

**Constraints from prior phases:**
- Phase 16 relocated all 32 declarations and removed the re-exports from `input/mod.rs`; the ids to write into the default file are the ones it chose.
- Phase 14 created `InteractionContext` deriving `States` + the strum traits; `for_state_context::<InteractionContext>()` reads `Res<State<InteractionContext>>`.
- Phase 12's `Plugin::finish` **rejects any shipped default that does not resolve** — a typo in the default file is a hard startup failure, not a warning.
- Phase 6: the file root is an **object** with `$schema` and `bindings`; blocks are ordered; later blocks override earlier ones at the same `(condition, sequence)` identity.
- Phase 7: a binding with a condition beats one without for the same keystroke, so `space` → `transport::toggle_playback` globally plus `space` → `dimension_lock::pause_tracking` under `dimension_lock` resolves with no ranking.
- Phase 9's `keymap.default.jsonc` companion is generated from these same embedded bytes and published `0444` as a read-only reference.

**Pending decision:** what happens to the 23 `bind_action_system!` call sites this phase strands

Actual problem:
This phase deletes `spawn_actions`, `spawn_switches`, `spawn_commands`, and `spawn_platform_commands`. `bind_action_system!` registers two observers — `On<Start<A>>` → trigger the semantic event, and `On<Event>` → run the command (`crates/hana_rubric/src/command/bind_action_system.rs` after Phase 4c; was `crates/bevy_kana/src/input/bind_action_system.rs:31-42`). With no `Action<A>` entity ever spawned for a `OneShot` command, the first observer becomes dead code while the second stays load-bearing, and Phase 11's Spec still asserts the opposite: "the existing `action!` → `Start<A>` → `bind_action_system!` → semantic event chain **stays** as the statically declared path" — which stops being true the moment this phase lands.

What exists now:
- 23 `bind_action_system!` call sites whose first observer this phase silently strands.
- This phase's Files list omits those call sites, though its acceptance gate depends on them.
- Phase 11's Spec carries a claim this phase invalidates.

What should change (two options):
1. This phase's Files list gains the 23 call sites, and the macro is split so the dead `On<Start<A>>` half is dropped at each — an explicit, verifiable deletion.
2. `bind_action_system!` keeps both observers; the `Start<A>` half simply never fires because nothing spawns the action. Zero edits, but leaves 23 sites of code that reads as live and is not.

Recommendation:
Option 1. Leaving 23 dead observer registrations behind is exactly the kind of thing the next reader treats as load-bearing; and since this phase is already editing keymap wiring, the deletion is cheap here and expensive later. Either way Phase 11's Spec sentence must be corrected to say the chain stays only until this phase, and this phase's Files list must name the call sites.

Approve this direction, or modify it?

**Acceptance gate:** `verify.sh check|test|lint hana` green. Every shortcut that worked before this phase works after it — walk the full list from the pre-migration `spawn_*` calls. **Two expected differences, both from Phase 11's modifier model, are not regressions:** a shortcut whose keymap spelling is `cmd-`/`super-`/`win-` now means the *physical* Super key on Linux and Windows rather than Control, so any migrated binding that must stay Control off macOS is spelled `secondary-`; and `ctrl-` bindings now match physical Control on every platform, which they could not do off macOS before. Check each migrated spelling against those two rules during the walk rather than assuming a one-to-one carry-over. Startup succeeds with no config directory present. `~/.config/hana/keymap.jsonc`, `keymap.default.jsonc`, and `keymap.schema.json` appear after first launch; the stub is not overwritten on a second launch. A user file overriding one key changes exactly that key. **hana's shipped `keymap.default.jsonc` validates against the `keymap.schema.json` generated by the same run** — `finish` resolves the shipped default against the registries, which does not catch a root or block member the schema forbids.

### Phase 18 — hana: held actions on `Binding::Custom`, and one owner for held state · status: todo

**Blocked by:** G2. (`G1` superseded — work on `init/hana_catalyst` in `/Users/natemccoy/rust/hana_tool_graph`, reconcile before dispatch.)

#### Work Order

**Goal:** the two hold-to-act commands are remappable through the keymap without respawning their action entities, and only one system tracks held keys.

**Spec:**

**The two hold-to-act commands** (R3):
- `ModifySelection` — polled continuously in `selection/click_select.rs:34` and `selection/box_select/drag.rs:54`.
- `FreezeTracking` — `Start`/`Complete` observers in `selection/dimension_lock/position.rs:50`.

Both appear in the keymap file and are freely remappable to **any single keystroke including modifiers** (`f`, `shift-f`, `cmd-shift-f`). Binding either to a multi-key sequence is rejected per-binding at load — Phase 7 already enforces this from the `Held` capability.

**Each keeps one statically spawned `Action<A>` bound to `Binding::Custom(id)`, where `id` is *read* from `CommandRegistry::custom_input()` — this phase creates no `CustomInput`.** The matcher writes that input's `ActionValue` before enhanced-input evaluates, so `Start`/`Complete` pairing, `require_reset`, and blocking remain enhanced-input's job. **Remapping a held key changes which physical stroke drives the custom input — not the action entity.**

**Catalyst introduced `ActionSettings { require_reset: true }` around `Keybindings::new::<ModifySelection>`.** Reconcile: `require_reset` changes whether removed bindings enter `PendingBindings`, and this phase stops removing bindings at all. Verify the setting still expresses what it was added for against the landed code, and record the finding.

**Held-key state gets exactly one owner: `hana_rubric`'s `keymap/runtime.rs`** (G3).

Catalyst's `sync_global_shortcuts_activity` — a `PreUpdate` system ordered `.before(EnhancedInputSystems::Prepare)` — currently does two things: deactivates `GlobalShortcutsContext` while a `hana_diegetic::ImeInputBlocker` session is open, **and** holds it inactive until every held shortcut key is released.

**Keep the first, delete the second.** The second duplicates the runtime's held-source reference counts and already-down-key inhibition. Two systems in two schedule slots each keeping their own copy of "which shortcut keys are held", with nothing synchronizing them: the runtime can see a key as released and start matching while catalyst's system still believes it is held and keeps the context off.

After this phase: **context activity answers "should this context see input at all"; the runtime answers "given input, what matches, and what is still held."** Different questions, no shared state. `sync_global_shortcuts_activity` stops inspecting keys entirely — IME open means off, IME closed means on.

**Files:**
- `hana/crates/hana/src/input/global_shortcuts.rs` — the two held actions' `Binding::Custom` wiring; strip the key-inspection half of `sync_global_shortcuts_activity`
- `hana/crates/hana/src/selection/click_select.rs`, `selection/box_select/drag.rs`, `selection/dimension_lock/position.rs` — verify the polling and observer sites still work unchanged (they should; the action entity is stable)

**Constraints from prior phases:**
- Phase 11 owns held-source refcounts, already-down-key inhibition, and pending cancellation, and writes `CustomInputs` values **before `EnhancedInputSystems::Update`**. `sync_global_shortcuts_activity` runs `.before(EnhancedInputSystems::Prepare)` — earlier — so the ordering does not conflict.
- Phase 11 verified `CustomInput`, `CustomInputs::register_input()`, and `Binding::Custom` are public in `bevy_enhanced_input` 0.26.0 (`src/context/input_reader/custom.rs`, `src/binding.rs:112`).
- Phase 11's reason for this design, from source: removing an action's `ActionOf<C>` resets it and emits `Complete` from `Fired` (`context.rs:369`); removed bindings enter `PendingBindings` only with `require_reset` (`:553`). Respawning entities while a key is held completes the held action and can immediately reactivate its replacement from the still-held key.
- Phase 16 declared both commands with the `Held` capability; Phase 7 rejects sequence bindings for them at load.
- **The `CustomInput` already exists — do not allocate one.** Phase 4b allocates one per held command during registry construction. Read `Res<CommandRegistry>` and call `CommandRegistry::custom_input(&CommandId) -> Option<CustomInput>` (`crates/hana_rubric/src/command/registry.rs`), building the key with `CommandId::try_from(ModifySelectionEvent::ID)` — which needs `hana_rubric::KeymapCommand` in scope for the associated const. The registry resource is inserted by Phase 12's `Plugin::finish`, which runs before `Startup`, so the spawn system can read it.
- **Do not register a `Begin`/`End` observer.** `CommandRegistry::initialize` already added one per held command; a second would double-write the same input.
- **This phase's "one owner for held state" means one *consumer*, not one writer.** Phase 11 counts two held sources — the physical-key matcher and Phase 4b's event observer — and drives the input from their sum. This phase wires `Binding::Custom` to that already-shared input; it does not arbitrate between the producers.
**Acceptance gate:** `verify.sh check|test|lint hana` green. Manual and automated: holding the bound key drives `ModifySelection`'s polled sites and `FreezeTracking`'s observers exactly as before; remapping either in the user keymap changes which key drives it, with no `Complete`/restart flicker; binding either to `g f` is rejected at load with the hold-to-act message; an IME session still suppresses global shortcuts; a shortcut key held across an IME open/close does not fire spuriously on close. `rg` confirms `sync_global_shortcuts_activity` no longer reads key state.

- Phase 4b owns the `CommandId` → `CustomInput` lookup; the hana-side `Action<A>` spawn reads it rather than allocating. Two producers write that input value — Phase 11's matcher for a physical key, and the command's own `Begin`/`End` semantic event for BRP, scripts, and tests — so this phase's single owner of held state sees one signal either way and must not special-case the source.

### Phase 19 — hana: the unremappable recovery chord · status: todo

**Blocked by:** G2. (`G1` superseded — work on `init/hana_catalyst` in `/Users/natemccoy/rust/hana_tool_graph`, reconcile before dispatch.)

#### Work Order

**Goal:** the recovery palette chord works when nothing else does, and a user who tries to bind it is told why they cannot.

**Spec:**

**The requirement.** `hana/docs/hana/tool-graph.md:175` requires the recovery chord to be unremappable, independent of `Ready`, and excluded from user key maps. **The chord is system-key + P — `⌘P` on macOS, `ctrl-P` elsewhere** (changed from `⌘⇧P` because hana does not print). Its job is to work when nothing else does — empty world, mangled graph, no `Ready` — because it opens the palette containing factory reset.

A keymap file makes this harder: the escape hatch must survive a user rebinding `cmd-p`, nulling it, **and** writing a file that fails to parse.

**Three parts (D8), as resolved by G3:**

1. **Registered in Rust unconditionally**, before any file is read and regardless of whether reading succeeds. Checked ahead of the keymap matcher; consumes the keystroke. **This is the survival property — it does not depend on the keymap system at all.** Catalyst already implements `OpenCommandPalette` on Cmd/Ctrl+P hand-rolled with `KeyCode::KeyP.with_mod_keys(…)`; the mechanism exists, so reconcile rather than rebuild.
2. **Documented in `keymap.default.jsonc` as a comment** (Phase 17 wrote the file), so it is discoverable by reading the file rather than the source.
3. **The loader rejects any keymap entry claiming it** — Phase 7 implements the rejection; this phase supplies the constant and the `Unremappable` declarations.

**`PermanentShortcutsContext` is the mechanism.** Catalyst's second input context — documented as holding commands "intentionally absent from user key maps" — **is** D8's mechanism and a better one than the design had. Rather than the loader special-casing individual chords, commands that must stay reachable live in a context the keymap system never reads. D8's loader-rejection survives as the diagnostic emitted when a keymap file names a command belonging to that context.

- `PermanentShortcutsContext` **stays in hana**; it is a `bevy_enhanced_input` context (marker component plus `Actions::<PermanentShortcutsContext>` spawn). It does **not** move into `hana_rubric`.
- What crosses the boundary is only the *declaration* that a command is unremappable, carried by `command!`'s capability field. **hana owns the binding; `hana_rubric` owns the refusal.**
- Mark every command in that context `Unremappable` in its `command!` invocation.

**Why not Rust-only and silent:** a user who binds `cmd-p` and sees nothing happen has no way to find out why. **Why not a protected entry in the keymap file:** then the chord is carried by the keymap system, so a catastrophic keymap failure takes the recovery path with it — the exact scenario it exists for.

**One canonical parsed keystroke constant (M1)** shared by the always-on handler, protected-entry validation, the generated default comment, and the tests. Not four string literals.

**Keep the protected list to exactly one entry.** Every protected keystroke is one the user can never use for anything else. The bar for a second: the app is unrecoverable without it — true of the recovery palette, nothing else currently.

**Files:**
- `hana/crates/hana/src/input/global_shortcuts.rs` — `PermanentShortcutsContext`, the always-on handler, the canonical chord constant
- The handler module owning the palette command — mark it `Unremappable`
- `hana/crates/hana/assets/keymap.default.jsonc` — the documenting comment
- `hana/docs/hana/tool-graph.md` — the remaining M1 work: the chord occurrences were corrected on 2026-07-28 (uncommitted on `main`); confirm the acceptance gate wording matches

**Constraints from prior phases:**
- Phase 7 rejects any sequence whose **first canonical stroke** is the protected chord (so `secondary-p x` can never complete), comparing **parsed, platform-normalized keystrokes, never raw strings** — left/right modifier spellings cannot slip through. It reports the stroke index and the reserved command. **The rule is unchanged by Phase 11's modifier model; which spellings collide is not.** The chord is `secondary-p`, so on macOS the colliding spellings are `cmd-p`/`super-p`/`win-p` (and `ctrl-p` is an ordinary binding), while off macOS `ctrl-p` collides and `cmd-p`/`super-p`/`win-p` are ordinary bindings meaning physical Super+P. See Phase 7's protected-chord section, whose own text predates the model change and describes the superseded collapse.
- The protected keystroke reaches `hana_rubric` through `KeymapPlugin`'s `.with_protected_keystroke(Keystroke)` builder call — see Phase 12's Decision A. hana owns the constant; the library owns the enforcement.
- Phase 7 also rejects any command declared `Unremappable` wherever it appears in a file.
- Phase 7: enforcement survives a parse failure because the consuming handler is installed before loading, and every previously committed generation already passed validation.
- Phase 1 moved `PlatformShortcutMode` out of `keybindings.rs` into its own module and Phase 4c relocated it to `crates/hana_rubric/src/platform_shortcut_mode.rs` as `pub(crate)`, so hana cannot name it. Platform normalization now happens inside `Modifiers::with_platform()`, and because `Keystroke::new` and `Modifiers::none()`/`with_platform()` are all `const`, the recovery chord is a plain constant with no `cfg!` and no runtime parse:
  ```rust
  // Phase 11 adopted Zed's two-bit modifier model: `with_platform()` is now the *physical*
  // Cmd/Win/Super key on every platform, so a bare `with_platform()` constant would make the
  // recovery chord Super+P on Linux — a window-manager chord, not Ctrl+P. The chord is the
  // `secondary` modifier: platform on macOS, control elsewhere.
  #[cfg(target_os = "macos")]
  const RECOVERY_CHORD: Keystroke = Keystroke::new(Modifiers::none().with_platform(), KeyCode::KeyP);
  #[cfg(not(target_os = "macos"))]
  const RECOVERY_CHORD: Keystroke = Keystroke::new(Modifiers::none().with_control(), KeyCode::KeyP);
  ```
- Phase 4a's `Unremappable` capability is set in the `command!` invocation.
- **A `command!` call site needs five names imported unqualified**, not four: `InputAction`, `Event`, `Reflect`, `ReflectEvent`, and `ReflectKeymapCommand`. The expansion writes `#[reflect(Event, KeymapCommand)]`, so bevy's derive forms *both* `ReflectEvent` and `ReflectKeymapCommand` by concatenation in the caller's scope. **`hana_rubric::prelude::*` does not cover this** — the prelude re-exports types only — 25 of them since Phase 5 added the condition surface and Phase 7 added `DiagnosticSeverity` on top of Phase 4c's keystroke surface (`ActiveCondition`, `Capability`, `CommandId`, `CommandInfo`, `CommandRegistry`, `ConditionName`, `Diagnostic`, `DiagnosticKind`, `HoldPhase`, `Keybindings`, `KeymapCommand`, `KeymapContext`, `KeymapLoadFailures`, `KeymapPlugin`, `KeymapSystems`, `Keystroke`, `KeystrokeSequence`, `MatchOutcome`, `Modifiers`, `ReflectKeymapCommand`, `SequenceMatcher`, and the three keystroke error types) — but **not** the `command!` macro and **not** `Event`/`Reflect`/`ReflectEvent`/`InputAction`. Write the five imports explicitly.
- **`command!`'s keys are positional and fixed**: `action`, `event`, `id`, `title`, `description`, and optionally `capability` — in that order, `capability` last. It is `macro_rules!` arm matching, not a keyword parser; a key out of order produces an opaque "no rules expected this token" error. Omitting `capability` yields `Capability::OneShot`. **`capability: Held` is a deliberate `compile_error!`** — a held command is declared with the leading `held,` marker and no `capability:` key, which is what gives its event the `phase: HoldPhase` field. `capability: Capability::Held` matches no arm at all (the generic arm takes a bare ident).
- **Every `KeymapCommand` impl must satisfy `Event + Reflect` and implement `fn hold_phase(&self) -> Option<HoldPhase>`** (`crates/hana_rubric/src/command/reflect_command.rs:15`). `command!` generates the method — non-held forms return `None`, the `held,` form returns `Some(self.phase)`. A hand-written impl that omits it fails to compile; that is deliberate, so a held command cannot register as silently inert.

**Acceptance gate:** `verify.sh check|test|lint hana` green. Tests: the exact chord opens the recovery palette; a keymap file binding `secondary-p` is rejected with the reserved-for-recovery diagnostic, as are modifier-order aliases and `secondary-p x` as a sequence prefix. **The colliding spellings are now platform-split** (Phase 11's modifier model; CI is Ubuntu-only, so the macOS half never runs there): on macOS `cmd-p`/`super-p`/`win-p` are rejected and `ctrl-p` is an ordinary binding; off macOS `ctrl-p` is rejected and `cmd-p`/`super-p`/`win-p` are ordinary bindings, since they now mean physical Super+P rather than Control+P. **The rejection rule itself is unchanged** — reject anything whose first canonical stroke equals `RECOVERY_CHORD`; only which spellings reach that value per platform has moved. **reload while a sequence is pending does not break the chord**; the chord works with a whole-file parse failure in place, and with no config directory at all. The protected list has exactly one entry. `tool-graph.md`'s acceptance gate reads `⌘P` throughout.

### Phase 20 — hana: BRP id resolution and the three palette route commands · status: todo

**Blocked by:** G2. (`G1` superseded — work on `init/hana_catalyst` in `/Users/natemccoy/rust/hana_tool_graph`, reconcile before dispatch.)

#### Work Order

**Goal:** external control can invoke a command by its short id, and a person with a broken keymap can reach the file that broke it.

**Spec:**

**A hana-specific BRP method resolving short declared ids.** Stock BRP `world.trigger_event` resolves via `TypeRegistry::get_with_type_path`, so it **cannot accept a declared id like `camera::home`** — it wants the full Rust type path. A hana-specific method resolves the short id through the registry's side table first, then triggers.

This is why D1 chose the bevy type registry over a link-time inventory in the first place: one table serves the keymap loader, the command palette, schema generation, **and** BRP. External control is central to hana's mission, and a link-time inventory would be invisible to BRP.

The stock dispatch path to follow: `TypeRegistration::data::<ReflectEvent>()` then `ReflectEvent::trigger` (`bevy/crates/bevy_ecs/src/reflect/event.rs:67`), exactly as BRP's own `process_remote_trigger_event_request` does (`bevy/crates/bevy_remote/src/builtin_methods.rs:1480`).

**Three palette commands (R5):** open the user keymap, open the default reference, and reveal the config directory in the file manager.

- D6 writes bytes to `~/.config/hana/`, which Finder hides. Before the first-launch stub exists there is no user file at all, and **after a bad edit the person who most needs the file is the one whose keymap is broken.**
- **These commands must not depend on the user keymap** — a broken keymap must not hide its own repair route. They are palette-invocable and reachable through the recovery chord, but stay **outside** the minimal degraded-mode command set `tool-graph.md` specifies for the recovery palette.
- The keymap-error surface shows the **absolute path** with an open/reveal action; the path is also logged as a fallback.

**Also worth copying from Zed:** a query normalizer (`normalize_action_query`, `command_palette.rs:48`) so a user typing either the authored title or the raw `camera::home` id matches the same row. Zed's palette label is a humanized rendering of the id; **hana's is the authored title** (R6) — but both should be searchable.

**Files:**
- `hana/crates/hana/src/` — the BRP method registration (wherever hana's other BRP extensions live)
- The palette command module — the three route commands, declared with `command!`
- The keymap-error surface — absolute path plus open/reveal action

**Constraints from prior phases:**
- **Phase 4b's registry surface is:** `iter() -> impl Iterator<Item = CommandInfo<'_>>` (sorted by id; carries `id`, `title`, `description`, `capability`), `title`, `description`, `capability`, `custom_input`, and `invoke(&CommandId, &dyn PartialReflect, &mut World, &TypeRegistry) -> Option<()>`. It retains **no** `TypeRegistration` and no type path — the type path is used to build validation diagnostics and then dropped. `None` from `invoke` is the "unknown id" signal this BRP method reports. Use `iter()` for the palette listing and for the id half of `normalize_action_query`. The BRP method resolves through that surface; it does not build its own table.
- **`invoke` can panic on a malformed payload, and this phase is where payloads come from outside the process.** It hands the caller's `&dyn PartialReflect` to the cached `ReflectEvent`, whose default `trigger` (`bevy_ecs/src/reflect/event.rs:131`) calls `from_reflect_with_fallback` — which **panics** when the value cannot be rebuilt and the type has neither `ReflectDefault` nor `ReflectFromWorld`. Held events have neither: `event!`'s field arm derives `Event, Reflect` only. So this phase must validate the BRP-supplied payload against the registration **before** calling `invoke`, or add a checked variant of `invoke` that returns a diagnostic instead of trusting the value. `clippy::panic` is denied workspace-wide; a BRP client must not be able to abort the app with a malformed field.
- Phase 9's `KeymapPaths` is re-exported publicly from `hana_rubric` — the three commands read paths from it rather than recomputing them.
- Phase 3's durable diagnostic resource derives `Reflect` and `#[reflect(Resource)]`, so BRP can read the current failure set during debugging; the error surface reads the same resource.
- Phase 16 chose the ids; the BRP method accepts exactly those.
- Phase 19: these three commands are palette-invocable and reachable from the recovery chord, but are **not** in the recovery palette's minimal degraded-mode set.
- **A `command!` call site needs five names imported unqualified**, not four: `InputAction`, `Event`, `Reflect`, `ReflectEvent`, and `ReflectKeymapCommand`. The expansion writes `#[reflect(Event, KeymapCommand)]`, so bevy's derive forms *both* `ReflectEvent` and `ReflectKeymapCommand` by concatenation in the caller's scope. **`hana_rubric::prelude::*` does not cover this** — the prelude re-exports types only — 25 of them since Phase 5 added the condition surface and Phase 7 added `DiagnosticSeverity` on top of Phase 4c's keystroke surface (`ActiveCondition`, `Capability`, `CommandId`, `CommandInfo`, `CommandRegistry`, `ConditionName`, `Diagnostic`, `DiagnosticKind`, `HoldPhase`, `Keybindings`, `KeymapCommand`, `KeymapContext`, `KeymapLoadFailures`, `KeymapPlugin`, `KeymapSystems`, `Keystroke`, `KeystrokeSequence`, `MatchOutcome`, `Modifiers`, `ReflectKeymapCommand`, `SequenceMatcher`, and the three keystroke error types) — but **not** the `command!` macro and **not** `Event`/`Reflect`/`ReflectEvent`/`InputAction`. Write the five imports explicitly.
- **`command!`'s keys are positional and fixed**: `action`, `event`, `id`, `title`, `description`, and optionally `capability` — in that order, `capability` last. It is `macro_rules!` arm matching, not a keyword parser; a key out of order produces an opaque "no rules expected this token" error. Omitting `capability` yields `Capability::OneShot`. **`capability: Held` is a deliberate `compile_error!`** — a held command is declared with the leading `held,` marker and no `capability:` key, which is what gives its event the `phase: HoldPhase` field. `capability: Capability::Held` matches no arm at all (the generic arm takes a bare ident).
- **Every `KeymapCommand` impl must satisfy `Event + Reflect` and implement `fn hold_phase(&self) -> Option<HoldPhase>`** (`crates/hana_rubric/src/command/reflect_command.rs:15`). `command!` generates the method — non-held forms return `None`, the `held,` form returns `Some(self.phase)`. A hand-written impl that omits it fails to compile; that is deliberate, so a held command cannot register as silently inert.
- **`command!` has no event-only arm.** Each route command declared with it also generates an `InputAction` type that nothing spawns. That is accepted, not an oversight — do not hand-roll the event to avoid it.

- **The palette filters on `Capability::is_palette_invocable()`** (Phase 4b, Decision E): false for `Held`, true for `OneShot` and `Unremappable`. These three route commands are `OneShot`, so they appear; held commands never do.

**Acceptance gate:** `verify.sh check|test|lint hana` green. Tests: the BRP method triggers a command by short id and rejects an unknown id with a useful error; the three palette commands work with a **deliberately broken** user keymap in place; they work with **no** config directory; they work after a companion-publication failure; the error surface shows an absolute path. Palette search matches both the authored title and the raw id.

### Phase 21 — publish `bevy_kana 0.4.0` and `hana_rubric`, migrate both consumers off git revs · status: todo

#### Work Order

**Goal:** the two library crates are on crates.io at real versions, and both consumers — hana and nateroids — depend on them by version instead of by git rev or an old published release. The plan's temporary pinning scaffolding comes down.

**Why this phase exists, and why last.** Phase 15's pending decision resolved to *pin by git rev, not publish*, with the stated reason: "Phases 4a–13 all shape `hana_rubric`'s public surface, so publishing now means republishing after nearly every remaining phase. Publishing becomes the right move once the plan is exhausted, not here." This phase is that moment. Nothing after Phase 20 changes either crate's public API, so a release here is stable rather than immediately superseded.

There is a second reason it cannot happen earlier. **`bevy_kana 0.4.0` is a breaking release** — Phase 4c deleted the `input` feature, the three macros, `Keybindings`, and the whole `keystroke` tree from a crate that shipped all of it in `0.3.0`. nateroids is the only crates.io consumer and has been held on `0.3.0` for exactly this reason (a constraint repeated in Phases 1, 4c, and 15). Publishing `0.4.0` and migrating nateroids are the same piece of work and must land together.

**Spec:**

Four pieces, in this order. Do not reorder — each later step consumes the version the earlier one produced.

1. **Publish `hana_rubric` first.** It has no intra-workspace dependency at all after Phase 4c, so nothing blocks it and nothing else needs to exist yet. Give it a real `version` (it currently inherits the workspace's `0.0.1`, which is a placeholder, not a release). Confirm the manifest carries `description`, `license`, `repository`, `keywords`, and a `readme` before publishing — crates.io rejects some of these as missing and accepts others silently as bad first impressions.
2. **Publish `bevy_kana 0.4.0`.** Its manifest is already correct as of Phase 4c: `description` reads "type-safe math and cascade values", `keywords` no longer claims `input`, and the README no longer advertises input wiring. The workspace member is already at `0.4.0-dev` — drop the suffix.
3. **Migrate nateroids** (`/Users/natemccoy/rust/nateroids`) from `bevy_kana 0.3.0` to `bevy_kana 0.4.0` **plus** a new `hana_rubric` dependency. Every `action!`, `event!`, `bind_action_system!`, and `Keybindings` import moves from `bevy_kana::` to `hana_rubric::`; every `Keystroke`/`SequenceMatcher` use likewise (Phase 4c verified nateroids references none of the keystroke types today, but re-check rather than trusting that). **Do not migrate nateroids to `command!`** — that is a semantic change and is not in this plan's scope for nateroids at all. This is the same mechanical import swap Phase 15 performed for hana.
4. **Migrate hana** (`/Users/natemccoy/rust/hana_tool_graph`, branch `init/hana_catalyst`) from the Phase 15 git-rev pins to published versions: `hana_rubric = "<published>"` and, if `bevy_kana` is still a hana dependency at all, `bevy_kana = "0.4.0"`. Phase 15's own gate establishes that `bevy_kana` may not survive as a hana dependency — it only does if something there uses `cascade` or `math`. If nothing does, remove the entry rather than versioning it.

**Then take down the scaffolding:**

- `.claude/config/release.toml` — bump the existing `[[publish_path_pins]]` entry from `bevy_kana = "0.3.0"` to `"0.4.0"`, and **add a `hana_rubric` entry**. The `hana_rubric` pin is needed the moment `hana_rubric` is published *and* any published crate takes it as a **normal** dependency. Today only `fairy_dust` does, and `fairy_dust` is not published (workspace version `0.0.1`, no pin entry, itself only a path-only dev-dependency of `bevy_lagrange`) — so verify whether the pin is actually required before adding it, rather than adding a dead entry. `bevy_lagrange` and `hana_valence` take `hana_rubric` as **dev**-dependencies, which cargo strips at publish time and which never need pins.
- Delete any `[patch]` sections, git-rev pins, or path overrides the plan introduced in hana or nateroids.

**Use the repo's own release tooling.** `/release <package>` is the established path here (`bevy_lagrange` and `bevy_kana` both release through it) and it already handles the `[[publish_path_pins]]` rewrite on the release branch. Do not hand-roll `cargo publish` and do not hand-edit the pin on `main` — the pin lives only on the fire-and-forget release branch, which is what `release.toml`'s own comment block describes.

**Files:**
- `crates/hana_rubric/Cargo.toml` — a real `version`, plus `description`/`keywords`/`readme` if absent
- `crates/hana_rubric/README.md` — create if it does not exist; crates.io shows it as the crate's front page
- `crates/bevy_kana/Cargo.toml` — `0.4.0-dev` → `0.4.0`
- `.claude/config/release.toml` — bump the `bevy_kana` pin; add a `hana_rubric` pin only if a published crate takes it as a normal dependency
- `/Users/natemccoy/rust/nateroids/Cargo.toml` + every file importing the moved macros or `Keybindings`
- `/Users/natemccoy/rust/hana_tool_graph/crates/hana/Cargo.toml` (and the workspace root if the deps are declared there) — git revs → versions

**Constraints from prior phases:**
- **This phase writes to three repositories.** `bevy_hana_rubric` (the two manifests + `release.toml`), `nateroids`, and `hana_tool_graph`. Each has its own branch, its own build, and its own commit. Do not batch them into one change set, and do not assume a green build in one says anything about the others.
- **hana work targets `/Users/natemccoy/rust/hana_tool_graph`, not `/Users/natemccoy/rust/hana`.** See the Delegation Context note — the branch there has already rewritten hana's input module, so any line number this plan quotes for a hana file must be re-read rather than trusted.
- **`bevy_kana 0.4.0` is breaking and that is accepted** (Phases 1, 4c, 15 all record this). `0.3.0` on crates.io carries `keystroke` and the `input` feature; `0.4.0` has neither. nateroids is the only affected consumer and migrates in this same phase.
- **Do not migrate nateroids' declarations to `command!`.** Phase 16 did that for hana's 32 declarations because hana consumes the keymap system. nateroids does not, and Phase 4c already established the rule for the in-workspace consumers: relocation-style import swaps only, no semantic migration.
- Phase 15 pinned `hana_rubric` by git rev and repointed hana's ~60 macro imports. **Those imports do not change again here** — only the dependency line does, from a git rev to a version. If a macro import still names `bevy_kana`, Phase 15 was incomplete and that is the bug to fix, not a new migration.
- **Publishing order is a hard dependency, not a preference.** `hana_rubric` has no intra-workspace dependency and publishes standalone; `bevy_kana` publishes independently of it. But a consumer cannot be migrated to a version that does not exist yet, so both publishes precede both migrations.
- `bevy_lagrange` normal-depends on `bevy_kana`, which is why `release.toml` carries that pin at all. Bumping it to `0.4.0` is required before `bevy_lagrange`'s next release, whether or not that release happens in this phase.

**Acceptance gate:** `verify.sh final` green in `bevy_hana_rubric`. Both crates resolve from crates.io at their published versions. In nateroids: a clean `cargo build` with **no** `bevy_kana` path or git override, and `rg 'bevy_kana::(event|action|bind_action_system|Keybindings|Keystroke|KeystrokeSequence|SequenceMatcher|MatchOutcome)'` returns nothing. In `hana_tool_graph`: `cargo build` green with no git-rev dependency on either crate, and `cargo tree -p hana -i hana_rubric -e normal` shows exactly one instance. `rg 'git =' ` across all three manifests returns no reference to `bevy_hana_rubric`.

**Not in scope:** publishing `fairy_dust`, `bevy_lagrange`, or `hana_valence`; migrating nateroids to `command!` or to the keymap system at all; any hana behavior change. This phase moves version numbers and import paths and nothing else.

## Deferred cleanup — after the plan is complete

Structural debt accepted knowingly during the run, to be addressed once every phase has landed rather than interleaved with feature work.

- **Split `crates/hana_rubric/src/keymap/document.rs`.** Phase 7 grew it to 1526 total lines with `mod tests` at `document.rs:1167`, so roughly 1166 non-test lines against this workspace's rule of 500 non-test lines plus independently testable sections. The legal cut is the source-position-tracking pass, contiguous at the end of the file: `SourceIndex` (`document.rs:735`) through `SourceIndexError` (`:1157`) — `SourceIndex`, `SourceLocation`, `JsoncScanner`, `StringToken`, `SourceIndexError`, roughly 430 lines with six of the file's tests aimed at them. **`SourceLocation` must be imported back into `document.rs` from the new sibling**: two `pub(super)` types that stay behind reference it — `ContextSource` (`:351`) and `BindingSource` (`:371`, which Phase 7 grew to hold two `SourceLocation`s, a key span and a value span). Move it to `crates/hana_rubric/src/keymap/source_index.rs` as a **sibling**, never `keymap/document/`, because `self_named_module_files = "deny"` forbids `document.rs` sitting beside a `document/` directory; `SourceLocation` and `StringToken` become `pub(super)`. This is *not* the `wire.rs` / `parse.rs` split Phase 6's Work Order forbade — that one separates the data shapes from the parsing logic, this one separates the two parsing passes.
